Your letter of 11th August to the editor of the Guardian
has been forwarded to us by the newspaper who do not themselves
have the information that you need.

I can confirm that the late Flt Lt John Watts RAF, who sadly
lost his life in last week's Tornado collision, is the John
Watts who was one of the air display organisers for the
Fighter Meet 88 held at North Weald airfield on 14th and 15th
May 1988.

Yours sincerely:

Defence Press Office [RAF]

24th August 1988

Thank you for your letter of 21st August. I doubt very
much whether I can be of any further help to you.

As you say, you have already taken up this matter with
Mr George Younger - that is to say at the highest level
possible in this Ministry. Clearly, there is nothing
that I can add to the reply that my Secretary of State's
office has already sent to you.

Yours sincerely:

Defence Press Office [RAF]

UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECTS (UFO's)

Thank you for your letter of 4 December 1988 requesting advice and information
on UFO's.

The matter has been referred to the Ministry of Defence (Air Secretariat) 2a,
the department which deals with enquiries of this nature and I hope you will
receive a reply in the not too distant future.

Yours faithfully

Flight Lieutenant
for Officer Commanding
4 December 1988,
Dear Sirs,

I am writing to you with the hope that you my be able to help me. For a
number of years I have been interested in the subject of Unidentified Flying Objects,
(uFO'S ). My reason for writing is that I have seen RAF Rudloe Manor mentione several
times in publications relating to UFO'S, Can you confirm if your establishment does
carry out research in this field? Should you be able to help me in any way I would
be very interested to hear from you as this is purely for my own interest as the
subject is one which I follow solely as a hobby. Thanking you for your time.

Yours Faithfully,

18 October 1988.

Dear Sirs, :
I am writing to you with the hope that you may be able to help me. For
geveral years I have been interested in the subject of Unidentified Flying Objects,
‘(UFO'S). Several months ago I wrote to you with regard to West Drayton's involvement

in the subject and you kindly sent me a reply for which I am most grateful. My reason
for writing ia that I am hoping you may be able to help me with regard to one partic=-
ular incident. There has been much talk over the last several years of an alleged UFO
ineident which occurred in December 1980 in the vicinity of Rendlesham Forest, Suffolk
in December 1980, I was told recently that an Unidentified target was tracked by sev-
~eral Air Force bases over the area in question, I believe that one of the tases was _
RAF Watton in Norfolk, and that the informtion was subsequently passed onto West Dra-
yton. Can you tell me if records from the time, (Deo,25-28 1980) confirm this? Should
you be able to help me I would be very pleased to hear from you as the subject of Uni- .
-dentified Flying Objects is one which I follow purely as a hobby and any information —_
would be purely for my own interest. Thanking you for your time.

Yours Sincerely,

\6"" pecember 1988

Thank you for your recent letter to the Chief of the Air Staff concerning the
subject of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs). T have been asked to reply on
his behalf.

I think you will find it helpful if I explain tnat whilst the Ministry of Defence
does receive and co-ordinate some information about UFOs sightings, cur sole
concern is to determine whether cr not UrOs present a threat to the security and
defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not
normally the case, the MOD makes no attempt to investigate or identify whatever
might have been seen.

Tne Ministry of Defence has no specialist department which is appointed solely for
the purpose of studying reports of UFOs and we have no staff who are employed on
the subject full time. {he sighting reports we receive, which usually are limited
to the brief details of the incident passed on to us by those who witnessed them,
are referred to our staff in the departments which are responsible for the air
defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports in the course of their
duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications we do not
carry out studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources to under~

take any indepth investigations or general surveys. T am sure that you will
appreciate that defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing security
of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being
so we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations, unless
a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange things
have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations could
probably be found for most of them. Possibilities that spring to mind include
satellite debris re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud
formations, meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles

to mention just a few.
Although it is not for me to comment on the American involvement, as we do

not liaise with them on this subject, I would like to assure you that there is no
question of attempting to cover up any incident, nor are we trying in any way to
obscure the truth about UFOs from the general public.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

1G December 1988

Thank you for your letter of 31 August to the Chief of the Air Staff concerning
the unusual concentric circles in a cornfield near Winchester that you saw, and
photographed earlier this year. I have been asked to reply on his behalf, and
as you might expect, I am the person that you spoke to when you first reported

the occurrence.

As you may know, the Ministry of Defence's involvement with the subject of
unexplained phenomena is minimal, in that our sole concern is to determine
whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of the

United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do. and this is not normally the
case, the MOD makes no attempt to investigate or identify whatever might have
been seen. I am sure you will appreciate , given that defence funds are
already fully committed to the continuing security of the UK against, primarily,
aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries, that we do not have the resources to
carry out any in depth scientific studies or surveys unless a clear threat to
our security had been identified.

The circles which you observed seem to be a fairly common occurrence in the

Hampshire / Wiltshire area, certainly I have come across them before, but I regret
that I have no definite idea what might have caused them. I can say with certainty, .
however, that the phenomenon was not caused by military or civil helicopters. We
also do not consider that the circles present a threat to the defence of the United
Kingdom. Nevertheless, I would like to thank you for the information and have
pleasure in returning your slides.

Thank you for your recent letters to the Secretary of State and the
Chief of the Air Staff concerning the tragic accident involving two
Royal Air Force Tornado aircraft on Wednesday 9 August 1988. I have
been asked to reply on their behalf.

From the evidence that is presently available it appears that the

aircraft were involved in a head on mid air collision at approximately

720 feet and tragically all four aircrew were killed in the accident.

At present it is too early to offer any explanation of the cause of

the accident, although a Board of Inquiry has been convened and when its
investigations are complete, the summary of its findings will be published.
The work of the Board is still at an early stage and although it has not
been established that the accident occurred as a result of any deficiency
in the existing Low Flying System, the investigation has recommended a
review of night low flying regulations to improve deconfliction.

We understand your concern about the safety of low flying, but would
like to stress that low flying is not as dangerous as it appears and
that safety is always our highest priority. Indeed, the RAF's accident

rate has been steadily improving over the years, and the rate for 1987
was the best for over twenty years.

However, we are not complacent about safety and are therefore grateful
for your suggestions on the use of radio communications systems to
"track" low flying military aircraft. As you say, RAF Tornados are
highly sophisticated aircraft but I would like to assure you that the
pilots who are trained to fly them are extremely competent, professional
and are well versed in the operation and handling of the aircraft.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

yes Sincarehy

Thank you for your letter of 11 October with the enclosed report
covering an unusual sighting witnessed on 7 April 1988.

I have now had the opportunity to look through our file but regret

that you describe.

As I mentioned in my last letter, I regret that we do not have the
resources to undertake in any in-depth investigations or general
surveys, but from a purely personal point of view it seems to me that
this could quite possibly have been a star which was momentarily
obscured by cloud thereby giving the impression of moving away at high
speed and then suddenly re-appearing.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Thank you for your letter of October 24th, in which you confirm that
investigators for the MOD did not visit our witness as described in my
letter of April 6th. : .

This therefore means then that either the witness is lying, or the
men were posing as MOD representatives. As there was another witness to
the visit, who in my opinion would not tell lies, then the conclusion
must be with the latter explanation. Who were the men ? Were they froma
government department of which you are ignorant of ? Just civilian
investigators posing as MOD operatives ? From somewhere else 7? What ever
the explanation, they knew details about the case which at that time
were only shared by a handful of people. -

I don't know what you know about UFO scenarios, but there are many
cases from around the world where civilian witnesses have been
approached by men wearing business suits claiming to be government
officials. They almost always have an intimate knowledge of the
experience, and never check out.

One last thing. In your letter you say that ‘your description of the
MOD identity card..does not tally with our present design,’ What exactly
do you mean by this ? Does it mean that the description tallies with a
previous design ? Clarification please, if possible.

Merry Christmas,

i

Thank you for your letter of 5 September 1988 which asked for any information
that we might have on an Unidentified Flying Object (UFO) sighting witnessed
on 1 April 1980 in the East Midlands area.

Before answering your query, I think it would be helpful if 1 explain that whilst
we do receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the sole
concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not UFOs present

a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge

that they do and this is not normally the case no attempt is made by MOD to
investigate or identify the objects. The sighting reports we receive, which
usually are limited to the brief details of the incident passed on to us by

those who witnessed them, are referred to our staff in the departments which

are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom and they examine the
reports in the course of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications we do
not carry out studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources to
undertake any indepth investigations or general surveys. JI am sure that you
will appreciate that defence funds are already fully committed from the Warsaw
Pact countries. This being so, we could not justify the use of defence funds
on scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK
had been identified. ‘

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange

things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities that spring to mind

include satellite debris re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning,

unusual cloud formations, meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft

at unusual angles amongst many others.

Moving to your particular question, I regret that a search through our file
has not revealed a report which corresponds to the details that you gave in
your letter.
As far as general release of material on the subject is concerned, you may find
it helpful to know that, although we are not able to release material "en blec"
because of the limitations of the Public Records Act, we are able to answer

any specific queries you may have about particular incidents which have occurred

recently, ie within the last 3 years. We can let you know whether we have a
report on the incident and if so, what it contains.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Dear Sir

I,am righting to you again, concerning a sighting that happened
on, Thursday the 27/10//88, at 9-30pm,at Huncoat between Burnley and
Accrington, Lancashire, at map ref long 76 by lat 30.

The three witnesses concerned, were traveling from Clayton le
Moors, to Huncoat over Winney Hill in there car, they were looking
towards the old Huncoat power station when they noticed an unusual light
formation.

They stopped there car and, all of them got out to watch this
object travel at a very slow speed, heading south east then changeing
direction,to north west which was directly towards them, as it got
nearer towards them, it shone a bright yellow beam of light down on the
ground,which lite the all area up.

T asked them what the height of the object was,they said less
then 800-ft, and what was its speed, they said between 10 and 15 miles
per hour.

As the object moved over head the, witnesses discribed the
object as being rectangliar in shape, due to the light formations, that
were on its edges, (I have enclosed a photo copy of a drawing of the
object,) which one them drew for me.

The all incident lasted 15-minutes, and the object moved off
towards,Great Harwood and vanished out of sight over the hill.

Yours Faithfully

COMPLAINT AGAINST LOW-FLYING AIRCRAFT IN UK AIRSPACE Sec.{AS)2

~ 4 JAN 1989
Dear Sir, FILE

With reference to my previous communication, dated 26 August 1988
with regards to our organisations enquiries into alleged UFO sightings
within the Midlands and North over the last year or so, I am sorry

to say thatI have recieved no achknowledgement or report onto the
complaint.

The principal of my last letter centres around sightings of large
triangular/wedge-shaped/delta-shaped objects with an assortment of
lighting configurations and usually two large white ‘headlights’.
These objects, generally seen by witnesses at night, are silent
(except for a low humming heard only when beneath the object) and
often very low, appearing over populated areas.

One terrified a lone driver one early morning at Overseal, near
Ashby-de-la-Zouch in Leicestershire in November 1986; another was
seen very low by two police officers one evening, on 27 September
1985 at Long Clawson, near Melton Mowbray, Leics. Recently,

a spate have occured in the Midlands and the North, inciuding a
family terrified by a very low flying object of the above description
at Abbots Bromley on 22 September 1987. Recently again, around
Stafford on 16-22 May this year many witnesses observed an object of
the same description. The local MP. the Rt. Hon. William Cash, is
now taking up the matter with the MoD.

A collegue from Sheffield Om has also contacted the

CAA (at Manchester, I believe) is also investigating similar
sightings in Yorkshire and more recently in Lancashire. A flap of
the triangular objects occurred over Rotherham and Sheffield between
7.30 and 8.00pm on Tuesday 2 February 1988.

Both EERO and myself again wish to make an official complaint
on behalf of the witnesses involved against low-flying exercises

over populated areas such as Rotherham/Sheffield, Stafford and
Ashby-de-la-Zouch. The danger of an accident is very high,
particularly in darkness_when the majority of the sightings have

been reported. Acopy of report and my own information
has gone to the CAA at Manchester and to the Rt. Hon. William Cash
MP, as well as to AS-2 at the Ministry of Defence.

1
We would be very grateful if you could acknowledge this letter and _.
the contents within it as soon as possible and to make the appropriate
proceedures to investigate these sightings of UFOs, which are, in our
considered opinion, a cover-up for low~flying exercises over
populated areas.

I look forward to hearing your reply and I thankyou in anticipation.

Yours sincerely,

Regional Investigations Co-Ordinator (Midlands) for BUFORA.

UFO SIGHTINGS CURRENTLY UNDER INVESTIGATION BY BUFORA MIDLANDS

Dear Sir,

I write to you with reference to my previous communication of

26 August, of which yet no reply or acknowledgement has been
forthcoming,although I do very much appreciate the work load that
is commensurate with Whitehall functions.

I wish to bring to your attention yet again the prevailence of
triangular/wedgedeshaped/delta-shaped objects which first arrived
in UK UFO data in 1977. ‘These objects are large’ slow moving with
the ability to seemingly hover but also capable of fast acceleration’
wide variety of lights, but often with two bright white ‘headlights’
and are silent except for low humming when beneath the object.

In the last year or so these objects have become common, mainly in
the North, Midlands and East Anglia, and recently there have been
spates of sightings over Northamptonshire, Leicestershire, Stafford-
shire, Yorkshire, and Lancashire. In May of this year Stafford was
inundated by many sightings of an object answering to the above
description and the local MP, the Rt. Hon. William Cash, is now
bringing this to the attention of your Under-Secretary (we are

assisting Mr Cash with information). My collegue, Section 40 er
is also making extensive enquiries into a ap of similar

objects over Rotheram and Sheffield, the main date of activity being
2 February of this year.

We are all concerned about the use of aircraft (possibly Stealth or
its varients) operating under the disguise of UFOs over populated
areas, particularly in darkness. Although witnessess and the press
discuss these sightings as UlGs we believe that they are aircraft
testing exercises and as such are performing dangerous operations.
If they are not RAF, then this surely brings into consideration the
possibility of USAF operations. Again, if they are not NATO
operations, then are we to suppose that Warsaw Pact forces are able
to slip undetected and unchallenged into UK airspace ? If they are
not, then are we to suppose that many witnesses are suffering
delusions, or even that we are being watched over. by some kind of
extra-intellegence whilst the military authorities stand helplessly
by ? The whole issue begs many questions and obviously answers.

PTO
I am sorry to present such a hefty dossier of reports and allegations ©
but I have been charged into following up the Stafford flap (and

other individual cases mentioned )*which all suggest, when taken into
consideration with other occurrances elsewhere in the country, that
low-flying operations over populated areas is going on, and that

some of these may represent some new form of aircraft.

I look forward to your reply and I thank you in anticipation.

Yours sincerely,

Regional investigations Co-Ordinator (Midlands) BUFORA

Date J8 November 1988

ov

Thank you for your letter of 1 September about the subject
of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs).

I was sorry to read that despite the assurances given in my
letter of 22 August you still feel that the Ministry of Defence
is trying to obscure the truth about UFOs. As I mentioned then,
we would suggest that rational and plausible explanations could
probably be found for most unusual sightings, and although you
say that a small percentage of incidents remain unsolved I think
it would be fair to say that as far as the MOD is concerned, the
majority of reports that we receive remain unexplained. This is
because we do not investigate or feel there is a need to identify
sightings unless a specific threat to the security and defence of
the United Kingdom had been identified.

You also mentioned that you had discovered a once classified
Air Ministry file on UFOs in the Public Records Office; however
I would like to confirm that the limited information that we now
have on the subject is unclassified - as you would expect with
sighting reports passed in by members of the general public.

Moving now to your request for any information that we might
have received concerning a sighting witnessed near RAF St Mawgan
on 6 February 1987, I regret to say that having now had the
opportunity to look through our file we were unable to find a
report which corresponded to the details that you gave in your
letter.

Lastly, as far as general assistance to the public is
concerned, I think I should stress that we do not have .the
manpower or resources to carry out general surveys of old files
and as IT have mentioned before the subject of UFOs is only one of
many tasks that I undertake as part of my total workload, and as
such consumes only a small proportion of my time.

I am sorry that I cannot be more helpful.

Aue Suerte

Unidentified Flying Objects Reporting (Short Title: UFOB)

WONTAR

1. Purpose and Scope. This Regulation es-
tablishes procedures for reporting information
and evidence pertaining lo unidentified flying
objects and sets forth the responsibility of Air

“Foree activities in (his regard. 16 applies to all
Air Foree activities.

2. Definitions:

n. Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOR)-—
Relates to any airborne object which by perform-
ance, aerodynamic characteristies, or unusual
features does not conform to any presently known
aircraft. or missile type, or which cannot be
positively identified as a familiar object.

hb. Fanihar Objects—Inchide balloons, as-
tronomical bodies, birds, and so forth.

3. Objectives. Air Force interest in unidenti-
fied flying objects is twofold: First as a possible
threat to the sceurity of the United States and
its forces, and secondly, to determine technical
aspects involved.

a. Air Defense. To date, the flying objects
reported have imposcd no threat to the security
of the United States and its Possessions. How-
ever, the possibility that new air vehicles, hostile
aireraft or missiles may first be regarded as flying
objects by the initial observer is real. This re-
quires that sightings be reported rapidly and as
completely as information permits.

b. Technical, Analysis thus far has failed
to provide a satisfactory explanation for a num-
ber of sightings reported. The Air Force will
continue to collect and analyze reports until all
sightings can be satisfactorily explained, bearing
in mind that:

(1) To measure scientific advances, the
Air Foree must be informed on experi-
mentation and development of new
air vehicles.

(2) The possibility exists that an air ve-
hicle of revolutionary configuration
may be developed,

(3) ‘The reporting of all pertinent factors
will have a direct bearing on the sue-
eess of the technical analysis.

4, Responsibility:

a. Reporting. Commanders of Air Force
activities will report. all information and evidence
that may come to their attention, including that
reecived from adjacent commands of the other
services and from civilians.

b, Investigation. Air Defense Command
will conduct all field investigations within the
ZI, to determine the identity of any UFOB.

e. Analysis, The Air Technical Intelligence
Center, (ATIC), Wright-Patterson Air Force
Base, Ohio, will analyze and evaluate: All in-
formation and evidence reported within the ZI
after the Air Defense Command has exhausted
all efforts to identify the UFOB; and all informa-
tion and evidence collceted in oversca areas.

d. Coaperation. AN activities will cooperate
with Air Defense Command representatives to
insure the economical and prompt success of an
investigation, including the furnishing of air and
ground transportation, when feasible.

5. Guidance. The thoroughness and quality
of a report or investigation into incidents of un-
identified flying objects are limited only by the
resourcefulness and imagination of the person
responsible for preparing the report. Guidance
set forth below is based on experience and has
been found helpful in evaluating incidents:

a. Theodolite measurements of changes of
azimuth and elevation and angular size.

b. Interception, identification, or air search

*This Regulation supersedes AFR 200-2, 26 August 1953, including Change 200-2A, 2 November 1953.

|

|

AFR 200-2
5-7

action. These actions may be taken if appro-
priate and within the scope of existing air defense
regulations.

ce. Contact with local aircraft control and
warning (AC&W) units, ground observation corps
(GOC) posts and filter centers, pilots and crews
of aircraft aloft at the time and place of sighting
whenever feasible, and any other persons or or-

ganizations which may have factual data bearing ©

on the UFOB or may be able to offer corroborat-
ing evidence, electronic or otherwise.

d. Consultation with military or civilian
weather forecasters to obtain data on: Tracks
of weather balloons released in the area, since
these often are responsible for sightings; and any
unusual meteorological activity which may have
a bearing on the UFOB.

e. Consultation with astronomers in the area
to determine whether any astronomical] body or
phenomenon would account for or have a bearing
on the observation.

f. Contact with military and civilian tower
operators, air operations offices, and so forth, to
determine whether the sighting could be the
result of misidentification of known aircraft.

g. Contact with persons who might have
knowledge of experimental aircraft of unusual
configuration, rocket and guided missile firings,
and so forth, in the area.

6. Z1 Collection. The Air Defense Command
has a direct interest in the facts pertaining to
UFOB’s reported within the ZI and has, in the
4602d Air Intelligence Service Squadron (AISS),
the capability to investigate these reports. The
4602d ATSS is composed of specialists trained for
field collection and investigation of matters of
air intelligence interest which occur within the
ZY. This squadron is highly mobile and deployed
throughout the ZI as follows: Flights are at-
tached to air defense divisions, detachments are
attached to each of the defense forces, and the
aquadron headquarters is Ioeated at Peteraon

Ficld, Colorado, adjacent to Headquarters, Air{

Defense Command. Air Force activities, there-
fore, should establish and maintain liaison with
the nearest element of this squadron. This can
be accomplished by contacting the appropriate
echelon of the Air Defense Command as outlined
above.

a. All Air Force activities are authorized to
conduct such preliminary investigation as may
be required for reporting purposes; however, in-
vestigations should not be carried beyond this
point, unless such action is requested by the
4602d AISS.

b. On occasions—after initial reports are

submitted—additional data is required which
can be developed more economically by the
nearest Air Force activity, such as: narrative
statements, sketches, marked maps, charts, and
so forth. Under such circumstances, appropriate
commanders will be contacted by the 4602d AISS,

ce. Direct communication between echelons
of the 4602d AISS and Air Force activitics is
authorized.

7. Reporting. All information relating to
UFOB's will be reported promptly. The method
(electrical or written) and priority of dispatch
will be selected in accordance with the apparent.
intelligence value of the information. In most
instances, reports will be made by electrical
means: Information over 24 hours old will be
given a “deferred” precedence. Reports over 3
days old will be made by written report prepared
on AF Form 112, Air Intelligence Information
Report, and AF Form 112a, Supplement to AF
Form 112.

a. Addressees:
(1) Electrical Reports. All electrical re-
ports will be multiple addressed to:

(a) Commander, Air Defense Com-

mand, Ent Air Foree Base, Colo-
rado Springs, Colorado.

(b) Nearest Air Division (Defense).

(For ZI only.)
(ce) Commander, Air Technical Intelli-
gence Center, Wright-Patterson Air
Force Base, Ohio. .
(d) Director of Intelligence, Headquar-
ters USAF, Washington 25, D. CL
(2) Written Reports:

(a) Within the ZI, reports will be sub-
mitted direct. to the Air Defense
Command. Air Defense Command
will reproduce the report and dis-
tribute it to interested ZI intelli-
genee agencies, The original report.
together with notation of the dis-
tribution effected thes will be for=
warded to the Director of Intelli-
gence, Headquarters USAF, Wash-
ington 25, D. C. :
Outside the ZI, reports will he sub-
mitted direct to Director of Entelli-
gence, Headquarters USAF, Wash-
ington 25, D. C. as prescribed in
‘Tntelligence Collection Instruc-
tions” (ICI), June 1954.

bh. Short Title. “TROB" will appear at the
beginning of the text of eleetrienl messages and
in the subject of written reports.

c. Negative Data. The word “negative”

Foreword JULY 1959

1. This pamphlet is designed to familiarize masters of
merchant ships and fishing vessels and radio officers with

the procedures for reporting vital intelligence sightings.

The reports are desired for early warning in defense of the
North American Continent, United States and Canadian Terri-
tories and Possessions. Timely receipt of reports of sightings
will enable military forces to take prompt defensive or
investigative action.

Dear Sir

On Friday the 30/9/88 some friends of mine, at different
locations were looking towards the Rossendale area, from Great Harwood
and also from Burnley, the time was exactly 7-15 pm when they noticed a
bank of very bright lights at map referance long 84 by lat 25.

These lights appeared to be stationery for ten minutes, then
they veered off to the right at very high speed towards Winter Hill.

They then just vanished; my friends' estimated their height to
be between 800 ft and 1500 ft, now as you know, this area is on an air
route and large aircraft don't usually fly at the kind of heights quoted
above, so I wondered if MOD had any explanation.

It may be something or nothing, but half an hour earlier, prior
to these sightings, at 6-45 pm, a women in the Clayton Le Moors area,
looking north towards Pendle Hill, spotted a boomerang type craft with
numerous points of lights all around its edges. ,

The object was moving north too south, to the above map
reference, it would appear highly probable that these two incidents are
connected, I would therefore be most grateful if you could offer any
further evidence to support this probability.

I look forward to reading your comments on this matter.

ours faithfull

Thank you for the copy of the letter to you from the Canadian
Department of National Defense dated 24 October 1972 which outlined
their policy on the phenomenon of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs).

With regard to the American orders that you mention, I regret that

as they appear to be instructions for American and Canadian military
personnel rather than for those serving in the Royal Air Force we do
not seem to have come into contact with them before. Certainly, my
colleagues in the Air Force Department have not heard of the orders that
you are interested in and I regret that due to the long gap since they
were issued, and because of limited resources,I cannot authorise what
may well be a futile search through Ministry of Defence archives to

see if we had received copies of them in the past.

As such I regret that we are unable togivea view except to reiterate
that there are no restrictions on UK military discussing details of
what they might have seen, unless of course it was thought to be a
Soviet military aircraft or similar. I am sorry I cannot be more
helpful.

Thursday, 21 April, 1988

UFOs ‘were planets’

UFO sightings over the
Surrey Hills last month
have been investigated by
Redhill and Reigate Phe-
nomena Research Group
who have concladed that
the strange lights visible in
the March Sky were the
plancis Venus and Jupiter.

The group spent six
weeks researching the
reports of the strange
appearance of two bright
lights in the sky during the
weekend of March 4 and 5
talking to witnesses, local
police. meterological
offices, Ministry of

Defence and the Civil Avi-
ation Authority.

A detailed report was
sent to the Royal Green-

wich Observatory for its,

analysis,

RRPRG spokesman Mr
Norman Hatt said: “The
position of the two lights
reported by the witnesses
corresponded exactly with
the position and the alti-
tude above the horizon of
Venus and Jupiter, as con-
firmed by the Royal
Greenwich Observatory.”

The group's report said
that: “Statements from
witnesses corresponded

with the movement of the
two planets.

Although witnesses
reported the sky was clear,
there was in fact a great
deal of cloud cover, says
the report. And Mr Hatt

THURSDAY, APRIL 28, 1988

Editorial: Phone Reigate 223411

Mystery lights
not a UFO says
research team

AFTER THE excite-
ment and uncertainty
of last month’s mys-
tery UFO sightings in
the borough, the
truth is out.

Bright white lights wit-
neszed by several people
between Dorking and Rei-
gate were not alien-bearing
spaceships but in fact the
planets Venus and Jupiter.

‘The news comes after six
weeks investigation by the
Redhill and Reigate
Phenomena Research

Group following numerous
sightings over the weekend
of March 4 and 5.

Group spokesman ' Mr
Norman Hatt said the poa-

ition of the two lights re- '

ported by witnesses corre-
sponded “exactly” with
the position and altitude

above the horizon of Venus.

and Jupiter, as confirmed
by the Royal Greenwich
Observatory.

The group report stated
that evidence showed the
lights moved from horizon-

tal to vertical positions,

corresponding with the
movement of the two
planets, Jupiter dropping
towarda the horizon as the
month passed, while Venus
rose.

The report went on to
say that at the time of the
sightings there was a lot of
cloud over, contrary to
witnesses who said the sky
was clear.

“This moving cloud may
well have caused the sud-
den appearance of the
phenomena and given the
lights the illusion of move-

ment " said Mr Hatt.

The information
gleaned from local police,
witnesses,  meterological
offices, the Ministry of De-
fence. and the Civil Avi-
ation Authority, was later
sent to the Royal Green-
wich Observatory for
analysis. The observatory
agreed with the theory.

Norman Hatt said the
group had not, however,
been‘ able to obtain confir-
mation of reported radar
sightings from official
bodies,

SIX YEARS after the end of the
Falklands War, the frst major
art exhibition devoted to, and
mostly ridiculing, the conflict
will open at the Manchester City
Art Gallery on December 10. [t
seems certain to offend many by
its overwhelming use of anti-war
material.

The exhibition includes work
by convalescing campaign veter-
ans alongside press cartoons and
satirical postcards. The post-

to reflect ironically on the Falk-

‘Union fiag: vest. Another pur-

missile.

ony

Thank you for your letter of 18 July 1988 to RAF Brize Norton which
has been passed to me for a reply. I am sorry that I have not been
able to respond sooner.

You asked if we could provide an explanation for an unusual sighting
witnessed over Indian Queens on Thursday 2 June 1988. I think it
would be helpful if I first explain that whilst we do receive and
co-ordinate some information about Unidentified Flying Object (UFO)
sightings, the sole concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine
whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of the
United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do and this is not normally
the case,and was not the case in this instance, no attempt is made by
MOD to investigate or identify what might have been seen. And, although
we are not able to say conclusively what the objects might have been,
our experience has shown that aircraft, either singly or in a group,
to be likely causes of a sighting of this nature.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications
we do not carry out full time studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we
have the resources to undertake any indepth investigation or surveys.

I am sure that you will appreciate that defence funds are already fully
committed to the continuing security of the UK against, primarily,
aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so, we could not
justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations, unless a
clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many
strange things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that
rational explanations could probably be found for most of them.
Possibilities that spring to mind include satellite debris re-entering
the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles,
to mention just a few.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Janae

Thank you for your letter of 30 July 1988 which asked for any information that
we might have on an unusual sighting witnessed in the Milton Keynes area on
Monday 18 July 1988.

I have now had the opportunity to look through our file but regret that I can

find no report which corresponds to the details given in your letter. Nevertheless,
I would tend to agree that there is likely to be a plausible explanation for

this sighting.

Taking the subject generally, I think it would be helpful if I explain that
whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the
sole concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not UFOs
present a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless

we judge that they do and this is not normally the case we do not attempt to
investigate or identify the objects. The sighting reports we receive, which
usually are limited to the brief details of the incident passed on to us by
those who witnessed them, are referred to our staff in the departments which
are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom and they examine the
reports in the course of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications, we do

not carry out studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources to
undertake any indepth investigations or general surveys. JI am sure that you

will appreciate that defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing
security of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries.
This being so, we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific
investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange
things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities that spring to mind
include satellite debris re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning,
unusual cloud formations, meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft

at unusual angles amongst many others.

I hope that you will find this helpful. 7 au sey pv +e cia an ~efgng.

Thank you for your letter of 11 August 1988 which gave an interesting account

of the unusual sighting that you witnessed whilst travelling in your car near
Colchester on 26 July 1988.

I think that it would be helpful if I first explain that whilst we do receive

and co~ordinate some information about Unidentified Flying Object (UFO) sightings,
the sole concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not UFOs
present a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless

we judge that they do,and this is not normally the case,we do not attempt to
investigate or identify whatever might have been seen.

The vast majority of information that we have on the subject is limited to brief
details of sightings that have been passed onto us by those who witnessed them
and these are referred to our staff in the department which are responsible

for the air defence of the United Kingdom and they examine the reports during
the course of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications we do

not carry out studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources to
undertake any indepth investigations, or general Surveys. I am sure that you

will appreciate that defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing
security of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries.
This being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific
Investigacion, uuless a clear threat to the security of tne UK had been identified.

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange

things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities that spring to mind

include satellite debris re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning,

unusual cloud formations, meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft

at unusual angles, amongst others.
As far as your sighting is concerned, I regret that a search through our file
did not reveal any other report which corresponded with the details that you
gave in your letter. Additionally, because we have not investigated this particular

incident, I am sorry that I cannot offer you a definitive explanation as to
the origin of the object.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Unexplained Lights

RAF/CC

1. Early in the norning of 27 Dec 80 (approximately 0300L), two USAF
security police patrolmen saw unusual lights outside the back gate at

RAF Woodbridge. Thinking an aircraft might have crashed or been forced
down, they called for permission to go outside the gate to investigate.
The on-duty flight chief responded and allowed three patrolmen to era-
ceed on foot. The individuals reported seeing a strange glowing object
in the forest. The object was described as being metalic in appearance
and triangular in shape, approximately two to three meters across the
base and approximately two meters high. It jlluminated the entire forest
with a white Tight. The object itself had a pulsing red light on top and
a bank({s) of blue lights underneath. The object was hovering or on legs.
As the patrolmen approached the abject, it maneuvered through the trees
and disappeared. At this time the animals on a nearby‘ farm went into a
frenzy. The object was briefly sighted approximately an hour later near
the back gate.

2. The next day, three depressions 1 1/2" deep and 7” in diameter were
found where the object had been sighted on the ground. The following
night (29 Dec 80) the area was checked for radiation. Beta/ganma readings
of 0.1 milliroentgens were recorded with peak readings in the three de-
pressions and near the center of the triangle formed by the depressions.

A nearby tree had moderate (.05-.07) readings on the side of the tree
toward the depressions.

3. Later in the night a red sun-like light was seen through the trees.

It moved about and pulsed. At one point it appeared to throw off alowing
particles and then broke into five separate white objects and then dis-
appeared. Immediately thereafter, three star-like objects were noticed

in the sky. two objects to the north and one to the south, all of which
were about 10° off the horizon. The objects moved rapidly in sharp anguier
movements and displayed red, green and blue lights. ‘The objects to the
north appeared to be elliptical through an 8-12 power lens. They then
turned to full circles. The objects to the north remained in the sky for
an hour or more. The object to the south was visible for two or three
hours and beamed down a stream of light from time to time. Numerous indivi-
duals, including the undersigned, witnessed the activities in paragraphs

I thank you for your reply to my letters regarding the
sighting over Saudi Arabia in February.

It is now my turn to apologise for taking up your time with
something which is not within your area of interest or
responsibility. In mitigation may 1 explain that I was under
the impression that all objects orbiting and decaying were
the subject of a monitoring programme - indeed we have had
circulars from time to time requesting details of specific
sightings when a large object has been in decaying orbit and
likely to reenter. It was the address of whatever
organisation this might be which I was looking for. Air
Traffic at Heathrow gave me yours when I mentioned it to
them.

I suppose now that I will never know what it was. I am sure
that it was not an aircraft of earthly origin and I do not
believe in little green men, so it would have to be a
reentry of something. Oh, well.

Yours Sinus

Thank you for your letter of 13 July 1988 which enclosed a copy of your letter
of 6 April 1988,

I was concerned to read that 2 men apparently under the guise of being representatives
of the Ministry of Defence visited your witness on 15 January this year. As

T mentioned in my last letter, unless we judge that an Unidentified Flying Object (UFO)
Sighting presents a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom

and this is not normally the case, we do not undertake investigations or seek

to identify unusual Sightings. As such we do not, nor do we have any need,

to visit individuals to ask them for an account of their experience. I am not

aware of any MOD representative carrying out such tasks and must deduce that

they were not bona fide officials from the Ministry. In addition, you may fing

it helpful to know that your description of the MOD identity card which was

used on the occasion does not tally with our present design. I hope that this

sets the record straight.

Lastly, in response to your letter of 13 July 1988, where you asked for details
of Government agencies which might have an interest in UFO research, I regret

to say that [ am not aware of any Government organisation, other than ourselves,
which has an involvement with the subject.

T hope that you will find this helpful.

Thank you for your recent letter to the Ministry of Defence about the subject of
Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs). I am sorry that I have not been able to reply
before now.

You asked for details of the numbers of sighting reports which we received over
the ten year period 1977-1987. Although details are not readily to hand for 1977 I
am able to provide figures for the remaining years, which are as below. In addition
we have received approximately 280 reports so far in 1988.

1978 - 750
1979 - 550
1980 - 350
1981 - 600
1982 - 250
1983 - 390
1984 - 215
1985 ~- 180
1986 - 120
1987 - 150

You also mentioned the “Education Programme" on UFOs which apparently concerns
itself with the gradual release of UFO information to the general public. I regret
this is something that myself and my colleagues are unfamiliar with; certainly, we do
not run such a programme here in the Ministry of Defence, not least because of our
limited involvement with the UFO subject, for which our sole concern is to establish
whether or not UFO sightings present a threat to the security and defence of the
United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally the case, we
do not attempt to investigate or identify whatever might have been seen. Since our
interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications we do not carry out full
time studies into the UFO phenomena nor do we have the resources to undertake any
indepth investigations. The information that we have on the subject is therefore
limited, usually to brief details of the incidents passed onto us by those who
witnessed them.
Although the concept of an “Education Programme”, wherever it may have come
from, is an interesting one, I think it would be helpful to say that that we in the
MOD have yet to receive any positive evidence to suggest that "alien spacecraft or
beings" have landed on the planet.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Yond Sanet

Thank you for your letter of 5 July 1988 which has been passed to me for a reply.

I was concerned to read that you believe that the Ministry of Defence knows

more about the subject of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) than we are prepared
to admit and would like to take this opportunity to assure you that this is

not the case and that we are not trying in any way to obscure the truth about

UFOs from the public.

On a more general note, I think it would be helpful if I explain that, whilst

we do receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the sole
concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not UFOs present

a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge
that they do and this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to
investigate or.identify any objects or unusual aerial phenomena. As such, we
have no department which is appointed solely for the purpose of studying reports
of UFOs and we have no staff who are employed on the subject full-time. The
sighting reports we receive, which are usually limited to the brief details

of the incident passed on to us by those who witnessed them, are referred to

our staff in the departments which are responsible for the air defence of the
United Kingdom and they examine the reports in the course of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications, we do
not carry out studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources to
undertake any indepth investigations or surveys. Indeed, it is because of this
limited involvement with the subject, that we do not, nor feel there is any
need to exchange views with the US Government. In addition, I am sure that

you will also appreciate that defence funds are already fully committed to the
continuing security of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw
Pact countries. This being so, we could not justify the use of defence funds
on scientific investigations unless a clear threat to the security of the UK
had been identified.
Lastly, you asked about the unusual sighting witnessed at Rendlesham Forest,
Suffolk in December 1980. The only information that we have on this incident
is the report by the Deputy Base Commander of RAF Woodbridge, Lieutenant Colonel
Charles Halt, USAF. In case you have not seen it before, I attach a copy of

the report, which may be of interest to you.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Thank you for your letter of 17 September enquiring about information held by the
Ministry of Defence on Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs).

Although I would like to confirm that the Ministry of Defence does receive and
co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, I think it would be useful to say
that S4(Air) was disbanded some time ago and the responsibility for the co-ordination
of information about UFOs has been passed to the Air Staff Secretariat - at the
address at the top of this letter.

Having said that, you may find it helpful if I explain that our sole concern with
the UFO phenomenon is to determine whether or not they present a threat to the
security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this
is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the
object seen. Similarly, since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we do not carry out studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the
resources to undertake any indepth investigations. I am sure that you will also
appreciate that defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing security
of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being
the case we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations,
unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

I regret that the release of any material on the subject "en bloc" is not possible
as, like other government departments, all MOD files are subject to the Public
Records Act. As you may be aware, this Act states that official files should
generally remain closed from public viewing for 30 years after the last action has
been taken. However, we are able to answer any specific queries you may have about
particular incidents which have occurred recently and can let you know whether we
have @ report on the incident and if so what it contains; but due to limited
resources, I regret that I can not instigate general searches through our files.

bons Svené
we

On a more positive note, you may find it useful to know that the Public Records
Office at Kew, Richmond, Surrey retains governmental files which are over thirty
years old and as such they may be able to help you further, although I am aware that
during the 'sixties' the MOD policy was to destroy UFO reports after five years on
file, which may account for a dearth of reports for that period.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Thank you Cor your letter of 1 July 1988 to the Ministry of Defence {Aic Force
Department) about the subjece of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOC). This has
been passed to me for a reply.

Taking the subject generally, I think it would be helpful if I first explain
Gur current policy on UFOS. As in the past, the sole concern of the Ministry

of Defence of the United Kingdom is to determine whether of not UFOs present

a threat to the security and defence of our country. Unless we judge that they
de and this is not usually the case, we do not make any attempt to investigate
or identify the objects. Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible
defence implications we do not carry out studies into the UFO phenomenon nor

do we have the resources to undertake my indepth investigations. I am sure

that you will appreciate that as a member of NATO, our defence funds are already
fully committed to the continuing security of the UK and its approaches against,
primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so we could
not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations unless a clear
threat to the security of the UK had been identified and as yet we have not
received any positive evidence to suggest that alien spacecraft or life forms
have landed on this planet.

information that we have on the subject is limited largely to brief details

of unusual sightings which have been passed onto us by those who witnessed them.
this information, including details provided by pilots, both military and civilian,
is considered to be “unclassified". There are no restrictions, therefore, on
military personnel discussing details of what they might have seen unless of

course it was thought to be a Soviet combat aircraft or similar.

ou also asked about our policy on the release of material on the subject to

the general public. Although you are correct in saying, that, in general, release
of material “en bioc" is not possible until a period of 30 years has elapsed, ;
this is not a policy directed specifically to our UFO files but rather a directive
covering Eiles from all UK government departments. The Public Records Act,

as it is called, states that all official files should remain closed from public
viewing for 36 years after the last action has been taken. However, despite
this, we are able to answer any specific queries individuals may have about
particular incidents which have occurred recently and can let them know whether
we have a report on the incident and if so what it contains.

i hope that you will Find this helpful.

Inne Sreonan,

Dear Sir,
Re: enclosed ufo report, reference number above, submitted for
your inspection and comment. .

Having investigated this ufo report without succeeding in
determining its stimulus, I am writing to you in order that, by
using your records, you might be able to shed some light as to
what the object allegedly observed could have possibly been. In
enclosing a copy of the relevant report, it is hoped that the
data therein will aid you in possibly identifying the object
seen as, possibly, an aircraft in the given area at the time.

Of course, I do realise that your jurisdiction vis-a-vis
the ufo controversy is strictly confined within the parameters
of defence, and this condition is taken as read, however any
information that you could supply to me, even if in the
negative, would be of immense help to me in the course of my
enquiries.

Looking forward to your anticipated response.

Yours faithfully,

Investigator,

INVESTIGATOR REF: Section 40) Maley,

BUFORA REF: pending

revest1¢aroR : <r

EVALUATOR: pending

CASE SUMMARY:-

OBSERVATION DATE(S): 7-4-88.

OBSERVATION TIME(S): refer to police video.

OBSERVATION LOCATION(S): no on-site investigation.

UFO CLASS: CEII.

INVESTIGATION COMPLETE: y/n/pending: pending

PRECIS OF OBSERVATION: -

Two police officers based at the Rickmansworth station notice/a
very bright star-like object in the sky and angularly close to
the planets Venus and Jupiter. So bright was this light, the
witnesses decided to record this phenomenon on their police
video equipment. After filming the event for some time, the
light suddenly accelerated away at fast speed, but however, its

place was taken by another light situated in roughly the same
location as the former.

A copy of the video tape is now in the possession of
investigator, Si’ and that all queries pertaining to
the tape shou e directed towards him.

deer Coprn.

Thank you for your letters of 22 February 1988 and 14 July 1988 which referred
to an unusual incident witnessed by yourself and 3 colleagues in the Gulf on
19 February 1988.

First of all, I would like to say that I cannot give you a definitive explanation
as to what you might have seen. It could, of course, have been an aircraft

of worldly origin or alternatively a meteorite or a satellite re-entering the
earth's atmosphere. I regret that I cannot be more specific.

More generally I think it would be helpful if I explain that our sole interest

in the subject of unidentified aerial phenomena is to determine whether or not
UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of the UK. Unless we judge
that they do and this is not normally the case, we do not attempt to identify

or investigate the sightings. You will understand, therefore, that we do not

see any need from the defence point of view to investigate a sighting seen in

the Gulf, however interesting it might have been. I am sure that you will

also appreciate that defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing
security of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries.
This being so, we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific
investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

I am sorry that I cannot be of more help.

iy por the delny wr negynr'e .

MINISTRY OF DEFENCE See
Secretariat(Air Staff£)2a Room Section Zi]

Main Building Whitehall London SW1A 2HB
Telephone No: ii!

Your Reference

Our Reference
D/Sec(AS)12/3
Date

7 October 1988

Thank you for your recent letters to the Ministry of Defence asking for information
on the subject of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs).

As | mentioned in my letter of 23 March 1988, the sole concern of the Ministry
of Defence is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security
and defence of the United Kingdom. The information we have on the subject is,
therefore, largely limited to brief details of sightings passed on to us by
those who witnessed them. However, because of the policy in force at the time
I regret that sighting reports received up to 1968 have been destroyed and as

a result I do not have any information on the sightings that you were interested
in. Nevertheless, on a more general note, you may find it helpful to know that
the Public Records Office at Kew, Richmond, Surrey retain§Governmental files
which are over 30 years old and it is possible that they may be able to help
you with further enquiries about events prior to 1958.

Moving now to your request to purchase any investigation that the MOD might

have carried out into the UFO phenomenon, I would like to remind you}jbecause

of our limited involvement, we do not carry out any studies or in depth investigations
into the UFO subject and, therefore, we are unable to provide you with any for
purchase.

Lastly, you asked about the unusual sighting witnessed in the vicinity of Rendlesham
Forest, near Woodbridge, Suffolk in December 1980. I take it that you have

seen a copy of Lt Col Charles Halt's report of the sighting; if not I attach

a copy which I hope you will find useful. From it you will see that the witnesses
described the object as being 3 metres wide and 2 metres high and that it illuminated
the area with a bright white light. In view of its reported small size, it

is considered highly unlikely that the object was a piloted vehicle. In addition,

I think you will agree that it is highly improbable that any violation of UK

airspace would be heralded by such a display cf lights. I believe that it is

equally unlikely that any reconnaissance or flying activity would be announced

in this way. As such we are satisfied that the incident is of no defence significance.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Paw Creed,

Thank you for your letter of 22 June 1988 tu the Commanding Officer of RAF Aldergrove
about the subject of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) which has been passed to
me for a reply. I am sorry that I have not been able to respond before now.

We were grateful for your photographs of the sighting witnessed during the evening
of 18 June 1988 and have passed them to our staff in the department which is
responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom. They have studied them
very carefully and have assured me that they could find absolutely nothing on them
for you to worry about.

You mentioned that in the past you used to send your sighting reports to S4(Air)

in the Ministry of Defence. This branch has been disbanded for some time now and
the responsibility o£ co-ordinating and receiving UFO sighting reports has been
passed to the Air Staff Secretariat - at the address at the top of this letter.

T should also mention that whilst we do act as the MOD's focal point for receipt
of UFO reports, our sole interest in the subject is to determine whether or not
UFO's present a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless

we judge that they do and this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by

MOD to investigate or identify the objects.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications, we do not
carry out studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources to undertake
any indepth investigations or general surveys. I am gure that you will appreciate
that defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing security of the

UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so,
we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations, unless
a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

\
Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that Many strange things
have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations could
probably be found for most of them. Possibilities that spring to mind include
satellite debris re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud
formations, meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles

or a host of other things.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Thank you for your letter of 18 March 1988 which asked for any information on
a sighting witnessed in the Reigate area of Friday 4 March 1988. I am sorry

i have not been able to write to you sooner.

T have now had the opportunity to look through our file and can confirm that
we received several reports which appear to correspond with the details given
in the newspaper clippings that you enclosed. Indeed one of them originated
from the witness featured in the newspaper articles. I attach
copies of these reports for your information, although in line with our policy
to maintain the privacy of the originators of the reports, their identifying
details have been obscured. I am sorry that 1 have not been able to obtain
better reproductions.

TI regret that we can give no definitive answers to what might have been seen
during that evening because unless we judge that a sighting presents a threat

to the security and defence of the United Kingdom and this is not normally the
case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects. However,
because of the mention of green and red navigation lights in one of the reports

we would suggest that the sighting is likely to have been an aircraft of some
description. Indeed, in general, we believe that rational explanations are
generally available for most sightings. Examples that spring to mind though

not necessarily all for this particular case, include satellite debris re-entering
the earth's atmosphere, ball lighting, unusual cloud formations, balloons, aircraft
at unusual angles, to mention just a few.

Since our interest in the subject is limited to possible defence implications

we do not undertake studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources

to carry our any indepth investigations or surveys. JI am sure that you will
appreciate that defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing

security of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries.
This being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific
investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Thank you for your letter of 28 February 1988 concerning Unidentified Flying
Objects (UFOs) which has been passed to me for a reply. I am sorry that I have
not been able to write to you sooner.

i was very interested to read the points that you raised but was most concerned
to see that you feel that we are in some way trying to obscure the truth about
UFOs. Please allow me to reassure you that there is no question of the Ministry
of Defence attempting to mislead the public or to threaten people to keep quiet
about the subject. Indeed, I must stress that the MOD does not and has no need
to, send out any officials to interview members of the general public about

UFOs and am, therefore, rather puzzled to find out that we are thought to do

so.

Taking the subject more generally, I hope you will find it helpful if I explain
that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings,

the sole concern of the MOD is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat

to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they

do and this is not normally the case, we make no attempt to investigate or identify
the objects.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications, we do

not carry out studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources to
undertake any indepth investigations or surveys. I am sure that you will appreciate
that defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing security of

the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This

being so, we could not. justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations,
unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

The Ministry of Defence has no department which is appointed solely for the
purpose of studying reports of UFOs and we have no staff who are employed on

the subject full-time. The information that we have on the subject is usually
limited to brief details of sightings which have been passed on to us by those
who witnessed them, They are then referred to our staff in the departments
which are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom and they examine
the reports in the course of their normal duties.
Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that Many strange

things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite debris
re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balioons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host

of other things.

Lastay, ou asked about the unusual sighting seen in the vicinity of Rendlesham
Forest, Suffolk in December 1980. The only information that we have in this
incident is the report by Lieutenant Colonel Charles Halt of the United States
Air Force and in case you have not seen it before, I attach a copy which I hope
you will find of interest.

Once again, thank you for your letter and I hope that this reply allays your
concern that the Ministry of Defence is being less than truthful about the UFO
subject. As I mentioned earlier, our involvement with the subject is very limited
and as such, we do not and more importantly, have no need to cover up the truth.

{ hope that you will find this helpful.

Thank you for your recent letter about the subject of Unidentified Flying Objects
(UFOs), which IT found very helpful and interesting.

I was also grateful for your kind offer of voluntary assistance, co-operation
and collation of information on the UFO phenomenon. However, as a member of
BUFORA, you probably already know (and at the risk of repeating it), that the
MOD's involvement with the UFO subject is extremely limited, in that our sole
concern of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that an unidentified sighting
does represent a threat and this is not normally the case, the MOD does not
attempt, as we feel there is no need, to identify or investigate the object
or objects further. The sighting reports that’we receive, which usually are
limited to the brief details of the incident passed on to us by those who
witnessed them, are referred to our staff in the departments responsible for
the air defence of the United Kingdom and they examine the reports during the
course of their normal duties.

In essence, therefore, I am afraid that we see no need from a defence point
of view to exchange data with an outside agency. Similarly, I feel that sponsorship
of BUFORA would not further the needs of the department.

I am sorry to have to give you a disappointing reply but would like to wish
you luck with your future endeavours.

Yours sincerely

Thank you for your letter of 15 June 1988 to RAF St Mawgan which has been passed
to me for a reply. I am sorry that I have not been able to respond snoner.

You asked if we could provide an explanation for an unusual sighting witnessed
over Indian Queens on Thursday 2 June 1988. I think it would be helpful if I
first explain that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some information about
Unidentified Flying Object (UFO) sightings, the sole concern of the Ministry of
Defence is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security and
defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do and this is not
nommally the case and was not the case in this instance, no attempt is made by
MOD to investigate or identify what might have been seen. And, although we are
not able to say conclusively what the object or objects might have been, our
experience has shown that aircraft, either singly or in a group, to be likely
causes of a sighting of this nature.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications we do not
carry out full time studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources
to undertake any indepth investigation or surveys. I am sure that you will
appreciate that defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing
security of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact
countries. This being so, we could not justify the use of defence funds on
scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had
been identified.

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange
things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational
explanations could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities that
spring to mind include satellite debris re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball
lightning, unusual cloud formations, meteorological ballons, aircraft lights,
aircraft at unusual angles, to mention just a few.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Dear Sir,

On the night of the 12th of September 1988 I was leaving the
Prince of Orange Inn situated on Carr House Road, Shelf,
Halifax. I do not drink as a rule and therefore I feel the need
to take this opportunity to stress the fact that I had not
consumed any alcohol on this night. As I walked towards my car
which was parked in the patrons” car park to the rear of the
building I saw a bright, sharply defined white ball of light
approaching slowly from the north west. I initially assumed that
this was an aircraft though I was puzzled by the lack of
identification lights which should have been clearly discernible
if the aircraft had been as close as the light had suggested.

I lost interest at the time, assuming it to be a helicopter with
a peculiar internal cockpit illumination configuration and
proceeded to unlock my car door. Just as I was about to get in,
there was a lull in the traffic noise from the A629 which was
behind me at this time and I realised that there was apparently
no noise emanating from the area of the sky occupied by the
object and I therefore concluded that any noise being produced
by the object was either not reaching me or that the object was
in silent flight which I know to be highly unlikely. It was this
above everything else which has led me to conclude that I may
have seen something peculiar. Though this feeling may simply be
due to my own misidentification of some form of aircraft I
should be most grateful if you could help me in my attempts to
identify and therefore solve this event which has, for some
reason, baffled me. I am especially interested in the pertinent
details of any one-off flight plans which may have been filed
for that night as I have checked that area of the sky at that
time on successive Mondays since the sighting and have seen
nothing similar. I have also checked the times and flight paths
of a few of the major commercial air routes and found that none
of them were in agreement with the time and flight path of the
object I saw.

The cases of three corroborating witnesses were cited in the
Bradford Telegraph & Argus on Saturday 17th September 1988 and
this had led me to conclude that the light did actually exist
and was not simply a figment of an hallucination or of my own
imagination. My health that night was perfect as it generally is
and I am not prone to bouts of hallucination pee. do I consider

uy

nay

imagination. My health that night was perfect as it generally is
and I am not prone to bouts of hallucination nor do I consider
myself to be in posession of an over~active imagination.

DATA

Date :- 12/09/88

Time :~ 21:05 p.m

Location :- Carr House Road, Shelf, Bradford.

Weather conditions :- Clear sky, moderate breeze, dry, cool.

Stars were clearly visible.

Object descriptions :~ Sharply defined white light with no
apparent structure pertaining to it.
Light did not vary in brightness and did
not alter shape.

Flight path :- Took approximately two minutes to cross a seventy

degree arc of sky in what appeared to be a flat
trajectory and moved in a south easterly
direction from the north western sky. No apparent
or major variations in speed and no deviation
from straight flight path in direction of
Huddersfield. Cannot estimate altitude or speed
as the sky that night held no reference points
such as clouds. Azimuth appeared be around fifty
degrees but I could not take a measurement as TI
had no equipment with me. The object was eclipsed
by the pub building. As I was late for an
appointment I did not have time to go to the
front of the building which faces south and see
where the object eventually went.

My professional occupation is in the capacity of a senior
Insurance Claim Negotiator and I have considerable experience as
an amateur astronomer and therefore would not be prone to
misidentification of aircraft unless they were viewed under very
strange conditions.

I thank you for any heip you may be able to give me which would
aid me in resolving this baffling situation.

Yours sincerely,
