Thank you for your letter of 15 February 1988 concerning the subject
of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs). I am sorry for not being able
to reply sooner.

I was concerned to read that you have reason to believe that the
Ministry of Defence is covering up the truth about UFOs and would like
to reassure you that the MOD does not try in any way to mislead the
general public nor do we attempt to obscure the truth about UFOs.
Indeed, we have yet to receive any positive evidence to suggest that
alien spacecraft or beings have landed on our planet.

On a more general note I think it would be helpful if I explain that
whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sight-
ings, the sole concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine
whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of
the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do and this is not
normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or
identify any objects or unusual aerial phenomena . The information
that we have on the subject is largely limited to brief details of
sightings as passed on to us by those who witnessed them. The reports
are then referred to our staff in the departments which are
responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom and they examine
the reports in the course of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications
and because we have no department appointed solely for the purpose of
studying UFO reports, we do not carry out any studies into the UFO
phenomenon nor do we undertake any indepth investigations. JI am sure
that you will appreciate the defence funds are already fully committed
to the continuing security of the United Kingdom against, primarily,
aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being the case, we
could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations
unless a clear threat to the security of the United Kingdom had been
identified.

Turning now to your question about the release of information on UFOs

I regret that the release of material "en bloc" is not possible
because, like other government departments, all MOD files are subject
to the Public Records Act. As you may be aware, this Act states that
official files generally remain closed from public viewing for 30 years
after the last action has been taken. However, we are able to answer
any specific queries you may have about particular incidents which have
occurred recently and can let you know whether we have a report on the
incident and if so what it contains. In addition, you may find it
helpful to know that the Public Records Office in Kew, Surrey retain
Government files which are over 30 years old and it is possible that
they may be able to assist if you have any particular queries.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Thank you for your letter of 9 August 1988 which asked for details on the sighting
witnessed at Rendlesham Forest near Woodbridge, Suffolk on 27 December 1980.

The only information that we have on this incident is the report by the Deputy
Base Commander of RAF Woodbridge, Lieutenant Colonel Charles Halt, USAF. In
case you have not seen it before, I attach a copy of the report which may be

of interest to you. As you will see from Colonel Halt's narrative the witnesses
described the object as being approximately 3 metres long and 2 metres high.

In view of this small size it was and still is, considered highly unlikely to
have been a piloted vehicle. However, because we do not attempt to establish
the identity of unusual sightings, unless there are obvious implications on

the security and defence of the United Kingdom, I regret that I do not know

what was witnessed at Rendlesham Forest. Nevertheless, I can confirm that there
is absolutely no evidence to suggest that anything, including Stealth aircraft,
had landed or crashed there. I would also like to assure you that the MOD is

in no manner trying to cover up any UFO incident nor are we trying to in any
way, to obscure the truth about UFOs from the general public.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Dear Sir,

NOTIFICATION OF LOW-FLYING INCIDENTS OVER NON-DESIGNATED AREAS

As the Regional Investigations Co-ordinator for the British UFO Research Association

(BUFORA), I am currently responsible for the investigations of a spate of UFO sightings
e

reported throughout the Midlamds in the last few months.

The majority of the UNKNOWN, that have been investigated seem to conform to a particular
type (known in oir UFO data since 1977), e@fectively that of a delta-winged or manta-ray
shaped object. Such characteristics that have neen described by witnesses regarding
this type include a silent motion (a low hummhng sound has on accasion been heard by
witnesses clode to the objects); a stow movement, although the object seems to have the
capability of making rapid movements and narrow turns; very low in flight; and two
powerful white headlights, and often a wide assortments of colourful lighting, of a strob

rype.

A brief selection of such reports include:-
OVERSEAL, ASHBY-DE-LA-ZOUCH, LET@ESTERSHIRE

November 1986. A man driving home early in the morning was terrified by a huge manta-ray”
shaped object with two white heaflights and a red light on the underneath. It flew across
his path and then shot rapidly away, showing its large underneath that appeared to
consist of piping and jointing.

LONG CLAWSON, MELTON MOWBRAY, LEICESTERSHIRE

27 Sept 1985. Two polive officers on duty observed a large structure cpmposed of four

white lights in a delta-shaped patter with a red light beneath. It flew silently (humm
ing sound reported overhead) and low. Official report produced.

ABBOTS BROMLEY, STAFFORDSHIRE

22 Sept 1987. Family of four terrified by slow-moving and silent manta-ray object with

a vast array of powerful colourful lights.
FERNWOOD, STAFFORD

16 May 1988. S,veral independent reports of triangular -shaped objects around 22.00 hrs.
Much local media coverage. BUFORA supplied data on the possibility of objects being
secret experimental aircraft. Bright searchlights emploed by objects. Stafford MP,

Bill Cash, currently involved in investigation. so

A spate of recent sightings has also occurred in Yorkshire, cur’ently under investigation
by the Independent UFO Network (IUN). Again, diamond-shaped objects, employing bright
lights and silent movements reported from Barnsley area on 10 Feb and 20 April 1988.

Our investigations, supported by avaition sources in the UK and USA, suggest that the
objects represemt a secret aircraft - Stealty. We are of the opinion that the F-19
fighte , and very likely its sister, the B-2 bomber, are operating in this country. There
is more than a shadow of a doubt that more advanced experimental aircraft and dirigibles
are being tested over populated areas throughout the UK, mainly, it would seem, in the
counties of Leicestershire, Derbyshire, ~taffordshire, Cambridgeshire,Yorkshire and
Northamptonshire. Although we mist maintain the security aspect of these aircraft and
their missions, we do believe that the British public deserves to know what is flying
around UK airspace, particularly so when such flights are operating at very low altitudes
Also, many of these witnesses are terrified by the sudden and strange appearance of these
objects, and since they employ very coihourful lighting comfigurations (in our opinion

to look like "UFOS", or something "alien" to suggest to potential witnesses that the
ohject is not aterrestrial craft under control by UK/USA authorities) people are of
the belief that what they saw was "other worldly". When they report such incidents,

it is more than likely that they will be informed by the authorities that the object was
not an aircraft and does not present a threat to UK airspace, and by extreme UFO buffs

as a "UFO" or "flying saucer".

As this matter appears serious, as evident by an MP taking the matter up, we hope that
you will respond seriously to our request to investigate these sightings as not merely

a "UFO report" but as an incident of low-flying and dangerous aircraft manoevres over
populated areas. If these incidents are not caused by Stealth or similar advanced air-
craft or dirigibles, can we have an investigation into these incidents as a serious
intrusion of UK airspace by unknown, and possibly hostile, foreign bodies? For too long
the authorities have been ignoring such reports merely because they have been described
as UFOs. If we forget the connotations with aliens(for which there is little evidence at
present}, and concentrate on the unidentified aspect, then perhaps we can have a serio us

investigation into such incursions over populated areas which are frightening witnesses.

I shall be happy to supply further information and to assist you further in your enquirie

into the matters raised above. Thanking you in anticipation.

Dear Sir,
UNSCRUFULOUS ACTIVITIES OF CERTAIN LOCAL CIVILIAN UFO GROUPS

Whilst I do appreciate the work load that is upon you in the course of your duties as

head of an MOD section ( and the large number of enquiries that are placed upon you

as the cléering house for UFO reports ), I feel that it is imperative that you be inform-

ed of the activities of certain civilian UFO groups.ti.t a7

As the Regional Investigations Co-ordinator of the Midlands for the UK's main UFO
research organisation - the Britsih UFO Research Associatiom (BUFORA), I am responsible
for the investigation and reporting of UFO sightings throughout the Midlands, and in
promoting a good image of BUFORA and ufology in general, to the public and the media,
as well as the scientific bodies who may be interested in our data and to the Mirlistry

of Defence.

Increasingly, for the past year the Midlands has seen the establishment of groups
purportedly "investigating" UFO sightings but who are, nevertheless, creating a bad
name for ufology, misrepresenting the public, and in fostering a misplaced image of

the Ministry of Defence.

I feel that it is my duty to make you aware of the activity of these groups /individuals
for two reasons: to distance ourselves from the activities and attitudes of these
egocentric people and their totally unscientific, wholly subjective and extreme methods
and ideas, and two, to present you with the information to handle such groups in the

future.

SE Formerly the

then very scientic and sensible. Since it split up iy has been taken over by
noted for Bis contact with the notorious SUNDAY SPORT. He has an excellent

1. ah

publicity machine and much contact whth the press and police whose reputation as a

man of extrem and chikish views they haveato realise. He has castigated serious UFO
researghers and the MOD and believes that we are undergoing a mas: of UFO alien invasions
He has the potential of speading much ill- conceived views and, perhaps, panics J
He has set himself up as the Midlands only UFO investigator and served to co-operate
with no-one. His investigation methods are non-existent. A serious note is that he is
using the practice of "investigating" UFO sightings as a way of making money. Every time
he investigates sightings (to him every sighting is an alien spaceship) he pesuades the
brainwashed indididual to join his organisation for £10-15, foe which they receive nothii
put a sdappy nesletter. The money, of which he has made a fair amount, goes into his own
pockets. His group, if it can be called that, does not have a committee nor trasurer,

and I believe this situation is illegal.

2

This has been in existence for about 2 years and again has a very good publicity
machine in the Midlands. Co-operation with BUFORA was offered but nothing materialised
from Section 40]] He is another one-man outfit who is “investigating! UFO sightings and~
doing nothing with the data. He is also creating illusions and misinformation on the

UFO phenomenon.

A serious aspect to this particular individuily is that he has set himself up as being
sponsored by British Aerospace (see one of his local newspaper articles attached).
Enquiries to this effect with him produced nothing but lies and when we approached BAe
they investigated and found out that they did not sponsor him. (see attached). Vhen =
Plas approached on this he responded with threats (see attached). Section 40

The issue warrants no further gomnet by myself, suffice to say that this man is
making the public and press believe that he is being sponsored by BAe ahd, by proxy,
is suggesting that the UFO phenomenon ( and himself}. is subject to official recognitioc

and fingncidla sponsorship.

I hope that the above is of interSt to you and that you may care to follow some, or
all of it, up in the appropriate manner. I shall be more than happy to co-opertate
with you on any aspect of the UFO phenomenon and to exchange data . Perhaps the MOD
could agree with a sponsorship deal with BUFORA , us supplying you with quality data
and subject matter. As an individual, however, I skelt be hapry to work with you in

any sphere.

Yours sincerel

RIC (Midlands) for the British UFO Research Association (BUFORA).

REPORT TO MINISTRY OF DEFENCE + UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECTS

RINGMARKS AT WINCHESTER 12] 2

unelosures : two photographic transparencies 22 mn DA
Boots colourslide film ISO 100/21
Camera - Voigtlander Vitomatic

Introduction. Wg Cdr GES) R.A. FP. (Ret'd) was driving
with his wife near Winchester and noticed unusyal
ringmarks in a cornfield below the hilltop road. ‘he ringmarks
were so strange that he stopped the car, walked back to
the hilltop where the marks were clearly visible, and took
photographs. the occurrence was reported by telephone

to Ministry of Defence Whitehall on 12 July 1988 and the
staff officer concerned with unidentified flying objects
asked that when available the photographs should be sent
there for study. Two photgraphs, which have only now been
processed as colour slides, are enclosed.

Date and Time. The ringmarks were noticed and photographed
on 9 July 1988 at 1630 hours.

Weather. The afternoon was clear and sunny with a fresh
SW'ly wind, after a rainy morning with cloudbase on the hilis.

iecation.....The ringmarks were observed from the A 272 road
which runs around a curved escarpment near Winchester,
between Telegraph Hill and Cheesefoot Head as marked on

the 1/50,000 Ordnance Survey Map. The ringmarks were at
point SU 529281. This point lies at 350 ft a.s.1. which
is two hundred feet lower than the escarpment road.

The land around the ringmarks was covered with growing corn
which was crushed by the imprint of the marks.

Description. The photgraphs illustrate the following features:

(a) The ringmarks comprised two concentric circles each
one metre in width, the outer having an estimated
diameter of thirty metres and the inner having an
estimated diameter of twentyfive metres. Within the
inner circle the cornfield was flattened over a circular
area with. an estimated diameter of twenty metres.
Overall the marks therefore had the appearance of a roundel
with a large centre and two thin concentric rings.

(bo) The concentric rings were noted as being very sharply
defined and although only one metre wide the cornstalks
in that one metre were completely destroyed while those
immediately alongside were untouched. All the concentric
edges gave the appearance of having been cut precisely
and mechanically.

(c) The inner circudar area was noted as being crushed
evenly over its entire area. The edges of this crushed
area were sharply defined as were the concentric rings.

(a) There were no tracks of any sort leading through the
standing corn to or from the ringmarks. There was no
diffused disturbance of the standing corn such as might
have been caused by downwash from a helicopter's rotor blades
There were no indications of a flight path sloping down
through the standing corn towards the ringmarks.
There was no sight of any flying machine either on the =. °~
ground or in the air which might have been consistent
with the ringmarks.

Inference. ‘The following deductions are offered:

(a) The ring marks were not consistent with any machine
used on the ground or with any flying machine deriving
lift from the air (viz., aeroplane, helicopter or airship).

(b>) The ringmarks were so clearly defined that details
of their configuration could have been precisely measured.
There can be little doubt that they were created by
vertical downward pressure from a very large mechanical
device.

) fhe mechanical device was assessed as being one with
a large flat circular base twenty metres in diameter
and with concentric rings developing a lift reaction.
This lift reaction destroyed the standing corn in two
narrow swathes forming concentric circles with diameters
of twentyfive metres and thirty metres respectively.

¢

QO

(4) Whe ringmarks were assessed as being the landing imprint
of the mechanical device suggested above.

(e) The reason for the landing imprint appearing on this site
is not apparent.

(#) There was no evidence of any hostile activity in
connection with the ringmarks.

(g) The site of the ringmarks is only twenty n. miles
from:AAEE Boscombe Down and the marks might possibly
have been made by an unconventional flying machine
associated with activities at AARBE.

Conclusion. The ringmarks represented the landing imprint
of a large flying machine descending vertically but without
any air reaction. The marks were not consistent with
present U.K. technology and constituted an unidentified
phenomenon. No hostile activity was indicated.
However the presence of the marks requires further investigation.

Wing Commander R.A.F.(Ret'd)
Authentication.
The reporting officer formerl commanded BERMEMScuadron
and later the Air Transport He entered
the R.A.F. through the Cadet College early in 1939

Your letter of 11th August to the editor of the Guardian
has been forwarded to us by the newspaper who do not themselves
have the information that you need.

I can confirm that the late Flt Lt John Watts RAF, who sadly .

Thank you for your recent letter about an incident reported in
your local paper involving anMOD Civil Servant working at
Shoeburyness New Ranges. I am afraid that we, as the MOD branch who
deal with UFO reports, are unable to give you permission to enter
Shoeburyness Ranges. as you request. We have, however, copied your
letter to Section 40, is free to contact you, should he wish to

discuss his experiences further.

Finally I might add that we have received no other reports of
this particular incident, nor do we have any suggestions as to what
it might have been although the majority of sightings can be related
to some totally natural phenomenon. Examples that spring to mind
include satellite debris re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball
lightning, unusual cloud formations, meteorological balloons,
aircraft lights or aircraft at unusual angles.

I hope that this is helpful.

UFO SIGHTINGS CURENTLY UNDER INVESTIGATION BY MIDLANDS UFO GROUPS

As the Regional Investigations Co-ordinator (Midlands) for the British UFO Researrh
Association (BUFORA)., I am cur:ently responsible for the investigations of a spate of

UFO sightings reported throughout the Midlands in the last few months.

The majority of the UNKNOWNS (the rest explained through natuagl phenomena and misident-
ifications of aircraft, satel. ites, etc) coliated so far seem to conform to a particular
type, effectively that of a dela- winged or manta-ray shaped object. Such characterist
ics as have been obtained from witnesses include a silent movement (a low humming sound
has on accasions been reported) and two very powerful white 'headlights', and an assort~

ment of colourful lighting on its body.

A brief selection of such reports follow:~
} OVERSEAL, ASHBY-DE-LA-ZOUCH, LEICS. November 1986. A man driving home early one morning
terrified by huge mants-ray shaped object that flew extremely low and silently over

car. Underneath was an array pf pipes and jointing and a red light. To bright front
headlights.

LONG CLAWSON, MELTON MOWBRAY,LEICS, 27 Sept 1985. Two police officers on duty observed
large structure with 4 white lights in shape of delta flyyslowly and silently over them.
Official report produced. ( Benmns , 9S pr

ABBOTS BROMLEY, STAFYS. 22 Sept 1987. Family of 4 terrified by slow-moving and silent

manta-ray object with a vast array of colourful lights. (Evcnany 19-20 p>)
FERNWOOD, STAFFORD 16 May 1988 Several independent reports of triangular-shaped ibjects

around 22.00 hrs. Much local media coverage with data supplied by BUFORA on possibility
of sightings being Stealth aircraft. Bright searchlights employed by objects. Stadéford
MP, Bill Cash, currently involved in investigations. : BEC EIVED

A spate of sightings have also occur ed over Yorkshire, currently under investigating

by sister group, the INDEPENDENT UFO NETWORK (IUN). Again, diamond-shaped objects have
been reported from BARNSLEY on 10 FEB 1988 and on 20 APRIL 1988.

Ourvinvestigations have suggested that what is being observed by witnesses conforms

to possible experimental technology, and our suspicions are on the Stealth aarcraft
(F-19, and possibly its sister, the B-2 pomber), or even more advanced experimentsl de-
signs. (see photostat of an artists'impression based on eye-witness accounts). Although
we must maintain the security aspect of these aircraft and the missions that they are
hndertaking, I do believe that the public have a certain right to know alittle of

what is flying in UK airspace, particularly so when s#ch aircraft is being tested/ on
active service in populated areas. Thepotential ofan accident is always there and it

is risking suck an accudent when these objects are witnessed flying very low. Also,
many witnesses have been terrified of their encounters with these objects, even more so
when they are perceived to represent "extraterrestrial alien sopaceships"because of the

cultural and emotional climate that links in with ETs.

Some of of sources do indicafe that either, or both, RAF Alconbury and Mildenhall are
host to these aircradt, probably being transported by Galaxy C-5Bs. Perhaps you could,
through your offices, be able to help us in our enquiries into these UFO reports and
suggest possible exp}aagions, and to comment on the Stealth link that these reports
seem to suggest. Are Stealth aircraft operating in the UK? If so, are there any safety
regulationsthat they must observe when they operate over populated areas? Why are the
Stealth displaying lighting configurations to make them look suspiciously like "UFOs"?.
If they are not Stealth, can we Bave a full MOD investigation into the .illegal and
threatenidg entry of foreign objects into UK airspace?

As it would seem like a serious matter , as evident by a local MP taking the matter into
his own hands, we are contacting the Civilian Aviation Authority as well in our request
to report such hazardous and frightening low-flying incidents. On behalf of BUFORA,
NUFON and IUN organisations, and the witnesses of these encounters, I wish to formally
issue a complaint into low-flying incidents as detailed above and to request the MOD

to investigate them, with a view to clarifying such low-flying activities and, in the
relevent cases, to prevent illegal and possible foreign objects entering UK airspave,

and threatening the security integrity thereof.

I shall be happy to supply you with further data on the above and I hope to be able to
co-operate with you on this and indeed any UFO -related matier in the fyture.

Thanking you in anticipation,

RIC (Midlands) for the British UFO Research Association (BUFORA).

Thank you for your recent letter which asked for any information
about Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs).

I think you may find it helpful if I explain that whilst we do
receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the
sole concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or
not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of the United
Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally the
case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the
objects seen.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we do not carry out studies into the UFO phenomenon nor
do we have the resources to undertake any indepth investigations or
general surveys. I'm sure that you will appreciate that defence
funds are already fully committed to the continuing security of the
UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries.
This being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on
scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of
the UK had been identified.

The sighting reports we receive, which usually are limited to
the brief details of the incident passed on to us by those who
witnessed them, are referred to our staff in the departments which
are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom, and they
examine the reports as part of their normal duties.

Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that
many strange things have been observed in the sky, although we
believe that rational explanations could probably be found for most
of them. Some that spring to mind include satellite debris re-
entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud
formations, meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at
unusual angles and a host of other things.

I regret that the release of any material on the subject "en
bloc" is not possible as, like other government departments all MOD
files are subject to the Public Records Act. As you may be aware,
this Act states that official files generally remain closed from
public viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken.
However, we are able to answer any specific queries you may have
about particular incidents which have occurred recently and can let
you know whether we have a report on the incident and if so what it
contains.

I hope that this is helpful. ue Frcwcs,

Jo.

Thank you for your letter of 5 July to RAF Marham enquiring
about a possible Unidentified Flying Object (UFO). sighting witnessed
py Becton 40 Kings Lynn, Norfolk on 15 February 1988. This has
been passe o me for a reply. .

As part of Skyscan you are probably aware that whilst we do
receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the
sole concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or
not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of the United
Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally the
case, we do not attempt to investigate or identify whatever might
have been seen.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we do not carry out full time studies into the UFO
phenomenon nor do we have the resources to undertake any indepth
investigations. I'm sure that you will appreciate that defence funds
are already fully committed to the continuing security of the UK
against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This
being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific
investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had
peen identified.

Turning to your enquiry about SEO sighting, I regret
that a look through our files has revealed that we did not receive a
report which corresponds to the details given in your letter.
However, I can confirm that the object seen was most definitely not a
refuelling hose, as these trail only a short distance from the
aircraft and would have undoubtedly seen the aircraft as
well. Another possibility could have been a Canberra target tug, but
again I regret that the descriptions do not tally.

I hope this is of help.

Dear sir/madan

I am an investigator for B.U.F.O.R.A and have been
following the case of the Rendelsham Forest UFO crash of
December 1980 for a number of years,I have contacted you in the
hopes that you could send me any details you may have on this
case.

I do know that through the freedom of information
act,certain documents have been released by the American
government of which I was able to obtain,I also have information
from colonel Malt,U.S Airforce who was at R A F Bentwaters at
the time.

I also have a number of reports from the general
public ref the said case which include drawings of an object
seen at the time.The drawings do not resemble a saucer shape but
are not unlike the top secret American STEALTH F19A reconnanance
fighter of which I have a picture.

My theory is that one of these aircraft crashed in
to the forest in December 1980 and to cover it up the story of
the UFO was circulated.

One of the reasons believing this is normaly the
American government and the M O D quash any rumours or sightings
of UFO’s as balony and put a veil of secrecy over the people
that have seen them.

Your comments and any further information would be
of great help,

I await your reply in anticipation.

yours sincerely.

DAMAGE TO CROPS

I understand from RAF Odiham that you have been concerned about
some damage to your crops in fields near Silbury Hill and believe
that the damage may have been caused by low flying helicopters.

As the Branch responsible for responding to complaints and
enquiries about low flying military aircraft activity, we have looked
at photographs of the damage which were taken from a helicopter from
RAF Odiham and would agree with RAF Odiham that the damage was
definitely not caused by helicopters. It would seem that the circles
have been man-made and deliberately sited to line up and form a
pattern with Silbury Hill, although we can't understand why.

I am sorry we can't be more helpful.

Yours sincerely

Further to my letter of 20 July I am writing to say that
additional information has come to hand concerning the incident in
the East Midlands on 9/10 December last year. I am sorry not to have
found these reports earlier.

Although you give no specific time for the sighting, the reports
we have found for the East Midlands and South Yorkshire areas
(bounded by Stafford in the West and Humberside in the East) cover
the period 5 to 6 o'clock on Wednesday 9 December. It is also clear
that your suspicion that the sightings could have been caused by
military aircraft were correct. I have established that on that
particular evening it is highly likely that the objects seen by your
witnesses were USAF KC-135 and Fi-11 aircraft which were on their way
to a refuelling exercise to be carried out over the North Sea.

I hope that this is of help.

Ihe report we have is of a large triangular shaped craft with altern-
ate red and yellow fixed lights along the edges of the triangle, but with a

not. used by the ahove stations.

from Brize Norton.

Thank you for your letter of 5 July which
enclosed a number of UFO sighting reports.

As you correctly say, our sole interest is
to establish whether or not the sightings
present a threat to the security and defence of
the United Kingdom. ‘As a rule, therefore, we
only examine reports which relate to sightings
which have been witnessed recently and which
can be considered to be current. Thus, whilst
I thank you for your kind thought, I suspect
that your reports will be of little practical
use to us. However I am sure that they will
come in useful when we need to answer other
peoples questions!

I hope that this is helpful.

Thank you for your letter of 28 June which enclosed a copy of an
investigation report of a sighting witnessed during the evening
of 18 June. You asked for our comments.

I think it would be helpful if I first explain that whilst we

do receive and coordinate some information about Unidentified Flying
Object (UFO) sightings, the sole concern of the Ministry of Defence
is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security
and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and
this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investi-
gate or identify the objects.

We have no department which is appointed solely for the purpose of
studying reports of UFOs and we have no staff who are employed on the
subject full time. The sighting reports we receive, which usually
are limited,to the brief details of the incident passed on to us by
those who' witnessed them, are referred to our staff in the depart-
ments which are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom,
and they examine the reports as part of their normal duties,

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications
we do not carry out full time studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do
we have the resources to undertake any indepth investigations. I am
sure that you will appreciate that defence funds are already fully
committed to the continuing security of the UK against, primarily,
aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so, I regret
that we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific
investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had
been identified.

Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that many
strange things have been observed in the sky, although we believe

that rational explanations could probably be found for most of them.
Some that spring to mind include satellite debris re-entering the
earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations, meteoro-
logical balloons, aircraft, to mention just a few.

Moving on to the sighting witnessed on 18 June, I can confirm
that we received rather a large number of reports for that
evening from the London area. Although some of the descriptions
of the object do not correlate exactly it is clear that something
was seen, and from the description given, it could possibly have
been a meteor burning up in the atmosphere. Although we have

not positively identified the object, I can confirm that we

do not consider the sightings to be a threat to the security

and defence of the United Kingdom.

I have pleasure in enclosing copies of the reports that we received
which appear to correspond to the details given in your letter

but in line with our policy to maintain the privacy of those who
send them in I have obscured their names and addresses.

Finally, although we are able to answer any specific queries you
may have about particular incidents which have occurred recently,

I regret that the release of any material on the subject "en bloc"
is not possible as, like other government departments all MOD

files are subject to the Public Records Act. As you may know, this
Act states that official files should generally remain closed

from public viewing for 30 years after the last action has been
taken. We are also unable to offer a general advisory service on
this particular topic, owing, I'm afraid, to a lack of resources.

I hope that this is helpful,

Yours sincerely

Re: “Radar Blip". FILE Ia}

Herewith copy of a narrative which I have recently received from a former
American Pilot for your consideration and comment. :

In the course of my investigations into this matter two suggestions have been
made to me, namely:-

A. That adverse weather conditions caused a "bogus" blip.

The witness concerned says the weather conditions were, apart from being
cloudy, “tranquil" and calm.

B. An experiment in "electronic warfare" was taking place by means of a
bogus radar pulse being transmitted to create an illusion on the Pilot's
radar of a solid moving target and that at the last minute before the Pilot
was due to release his salvo of rockets the target was very swiftly removed
off his radar screen by some technical means creating the further illusion
that the target had outrun the Pilot.

The Pilot concerned concedes that this is possible but does not think it
probable for a variety of reasons which were too technical for me to
comprehend fully.

Is it possible for you to say whether, in fact, 31 years after the event,
such an experiment was taking place using the Pilot as a “guinea pig*and if
it was not whether, in-deed, the technology existed in 1957 to create such
radar illusions.

The nature of my interest, and that of the Pilot, is to establish whether or
not there was a genuine large Unidentified Flying Object exggmmbesé flying

at speed and performing maneuvers beyond the capability of any known Aircraft -
on the other hand if the event was part of what was then part of a Top Secret
Military exercise and is still Top Secret perhaps you would simply say so and
confirm that whatever was involved it was not an Unidentified Flying Object
i.e. that your people know what it was and were in full control of the

situation even though you cannot say for security reasons;be it 31 years
after the event, wes jong Ove.

Thanking you in anticipation.

Yours fai
| fit was a typical English night in Kent. The 406th Fighter Interceptor Wing had committed to
3? Met sector (RAF) to have F-86D’s stand alert as an operational requirement. The date was
@ May 20, 1957, and our squadrons were considered combat qualified when they committed us to
the operational requirement. My recollection seems to indicate that this function was rotated
about England between the various RAF and USAF onits. This particular night the 514th
Fighter Interceptor had the alert duty. “Two F-86D's were on § minute alert at the end of the
runway at RAF Station Manston awaiting the signal to scramble. The hour -was late as memory
serves me and the weather was IFR. Looking back at the log book, & total of 30 minutes of
Night Weather was logged on a 1 hour and 15 minute flight. The details such as exactly what
hour the scramble occurred or what we were doing just prior to scramble totally escapes me,
however, the Auxiliary Power Units (APU) were on and the power was transmitted to the
aircraft. We were ready for an immediate scramble and eager for the flight time.

I can remember the call to scramble quite clearly, however, I cannot remember specifics such as
the actual vector to turn to after take off. We were airborne well within the 5 minutes
allotted to us and basically scrambled to about ight devel 310. Our vector took us out over
the North Sea just east of east Anglia. Seat the other member of the set

_ pf two fighters would be the lead ship. I can only suggest that I was jeading due to an in
place turn of some sort. I remember in quite specific terms talking as lead ship to the GCI site
{who's call sign I cannot recall), % was advised of the situation quite clearly. The initial
briefing indicated that the ground was observing for 2 considerable time a blip that was orbiting
the East Anglia area. There was very little movement and from my conversation with the GCI
all the normal procedures of checking with all the controlling agencies revealed that this was an
unidentified flying object with very unusual flight patterns. In the initial briefing it was
suggested to us that the bogey actually was motionless for Jong intervals.

The instructions came to go “gate” to expedite the intercept. Gate was the term used to use
maximum power (in the case of the F-86D that meant full afterburner) and to proceed to an
Jnitial Point at about 32,000 feet. By this time my radar was on and J was jooking prematurely
for the bogey. The instructions came to report any visual observations, to which I replied "I'm
in the soup and it is impossible to see anything!" The weather was probably high alto stratus,
but between being over the North Sea and in the weather, no frame of reference was available.
i.e. no stars, no lights, no silhouettes, in short nothing. GCI continued the vectoring and the

dialogue describing the strange antics of the UFO.

The exact turns and maneuvers they gave me were all predicated to reach some theoretical point
for a lead collision course type rocket release. I can remember reaching the level off and
requesting to come out of afterburner only to be told to stay in afterburner. It wasn’t very
much later that I noticed mty indicated mach number was about .92. This is about as fast as
the F-86D could go straight and level.

Then the order came to fire a full salvo of rockets at the UFO. I was only a Lieutenant and
very much aware of the gravity of the situation. To be quite candid 1 almost shit my pants!
At any rate I had my hands full trying to fly, search for bogeys, and now selecting a hot load
on the switches. I asked for authentication of the order to fire, and I received it. This
further complicated my difficulty as the matrix of tetters and numbers to find the correct
guthentication was on a piece of printed paper about 5 by 8 inches, with the print not much
bigger than normal type. It was totally black, and the lights were down for night flying. 1
vsed my flashlight, still trying to fly and watch my radar. To put it quite candidly I felt very

. much like a one legged man in an ass kicking contest.

The authentication was valid, and 1 selected 24 rockets to salvo. I wasn't paying too much
attention t put I clearly remember him giving @ *Roger" to all the transmissions. Y can
only suppose he was as busy as I was.

The final turn was given, and instructions were give to look 30 degrees to the Port for my
bogey. I did not have a hard time at all. “There it was exactly where I was told it would be at
30 degrees and at 15 miles. The blip was burning a hole in the radar with its incredible
intensity. It was similar to a blip I had received from B-52’s and seemed to be a magnet of
light. These things I remember very clearly. 1 san the range gate marker over the blip, and
the jizzle band faded as the marker super imposed over the blip. I had a lock on that had the

*_ proportions of a fiying aircraft carrier. By that 1 mean the return on the radar was so strong

what it could not be pverlooked by the fire control system on the F-86D. I use in comparison
.sother fighter aircraft and airliners. ‘The airliner is easy to get a fock on while the fighter not
--Speing @ good return is very difficult and, on that type aircraft, @ dock on was only possible
ander 10 miles. The iarger the airplane the easier the lock on. This blip almost locked itself.
Y cannot explain to the lay person exactly what I mean, save to say that it was the best target
I could ever remember locking on to. J had Jocked on in just a few seconds, and I Jocked on
exactly 15 miles which was the maximum range for lock on. I called to the GCI "Judy", which
signified that I would take all further steering information from my radar computer.

Let me explain visually what I.saw on my radar screen. Once tock on is accomplished, two
“#pircles of dJight appear on the screen. 30ne was a pomplete circle in the center of the radar
ecreen about an inch in diameter, the other about 3 inches in diameter with a half inch segment
darkened to indicate the overtake speed. If the dark segment was at 12 o'clock it meant 0
. overtake. If the segment was at 6 o'clock, then we had about 600 knots of overtake. The

"maximum overtake was in :the 9 o'clock position. The overtake I had on this particular

intercept was in the 7 or 8 o'clock position which indicate close to 800 knot overtake. 1 was
really hauling coals. To complete the description of the radar scope there were two other
significant pieces of data displayed. One is the horizontal indicator which gave a gyro
stabilized reference to the horizon enabling the pilot to not have to refer to his flight
instruments. The second is a steering dot, which was nothing more than computer data
indicating which way the aircraft should fly to accomplish the intercept. ie. if the dot was
above the center, the stick should be pulled back to climb, if it was to the right then turn to
the right to center the dot. The idea was to have the dot centered in the smaller circle.

A normal intercept proceeds from the lock on phase with the constant maneuvering to center
the dot. When the aircraft is in a position to accomplish its intercept, the dot would be
centered. The outer circle will start to shrink at 20 seconds from rocket release. The circle in
the center shrinks to about a quarter inch, and keeping the dot centered requires small rapid
maneuvers. At about the time the outer circle reaches three quarters of an inch in diameter a
small quarter inch line appears in lieu of the inner circle. This is the signal to puli the trigger
for rocket release, and to make only up and down corrections as the computer calculates the
point of rocket release for the azimuth. With the trigger pulled and the switches set, the
rockets are released by the computer.

Now back to the intercept of the UFO. As I said I had an overtake of 800 knots and my radar
was rock stable. The dot was centered and only the slightest corrections were necessary. This
was a very fast intercept and the circle started to shrink. I called “20 seconds" and the GCI
indicated he was standing by. The overtake was still indicating in the 7 or 8 o'clock position.
At about 10 seconds to go, I noticed that the overtake position was changing its position. It
moved rapidly to the 6 o'clock then 3 o'clock then 12 o'clock and finally rested about the 11
o'clock position. This indicated a negative overtake of 200 knots (the maximum negative
overtake displayed). There was no way of knowing of what the actual speed of the UFO was as
he could be traveling at very high mach numbers and I would only see the 200 knot negative
overtake. The circle, which was down to about an inch and a half in diameter, started to open
up rapidly, Within seconds it was back to 3 inches in diameter, and the blip was visible in the
blackened jizzle band moving up the scope. This meant that it was going away from me. I
reported this to the GCI site and they replied by asking “Do you have a Tally Ho?" 1 replied
that 1 was still in the soup and could see nothing. By this time the UFO had broke lock and I

‘Back in the alert tent ¥ talked to Met sector. “Whey ‘advised me that the blip had gone off the ~~

"ait was the next day before anyone showed up. oo ; :

¢ hall way around the side of the pilots ‘briefing room. He approached e civilian, «who

lifted in this day of enlightenment and all of us can have all the facts. This is my account to
the best of my memory.

Thank you for your letter of 28 March to RAF Uxbridge enquiring about
possible Unidentified Flying Object (UFO) sightings in the Castle
Donnington to Spalding areas on the night of 9/10 December 1987.

This has been passed to me for a reply.

I think it would be helpful if I first explain that whilst we do
receive and coordinate some information about UFO sightings, the

sole concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether

or not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of the United
Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally the
case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications
I regret that we do not carry out full time studies into the UFO
phenomenon nor do we have the resources to undertake any indepth
investigations. I am sure that you will appreciate that defence funds
are already fully committed to the continuing security of the UK
against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries, This
being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific
investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had
been identified.

Reports of sightings that we do recéive, which are usually limited to
the brief details of the incident passed on to us by those who
witnessed them, are referred’ to our staff in the departments which

are responsible for the defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine
the reports as part of their normal duties.

Moving on to your specific question, I regret that we did not receive
any reports which correspond to the details given in your letter.
Without knowing further details as to the exact times of the sighting
it is difficult for me to establish whether or not military aircraft
could have been responsible for the unusual incidents. However, we
too have found that military aircraft exercises have, on occasion in
the past been mistaken for unidentified objects.

I hope that'this is helpful.

Dear Sir,
Re-entry Sightings

IT wrote to you on the 22nd February last regarding a
sighting which my crew and I took to be a re-entry of a very
large object. It was a most impressive sight, almost
frightening at the time. We can understand a little more
sympathetically how Orson Welles’ listeners felt when he
made his famous War of the Worlds broadcast.

I had hoped for an acknowledgement if not an explanation of
this phenomenon but have had none so far. In case you did
not receive my letter I attach the text below.

letter dated 22nd February 1988

The following is a description of a sighting made by four
flight deck members, including myself, on the 19th February.
The position was taken from the inertial navigation system
and the subjective description of the phenomenon is from
notes made by me at the time and agreed by the other three
crew members,

date: 19, 02,88
position: WN 29° 37.2’ E 043° 29,97
time: 2352 GMT

aircraft heading 290° flight level 310

T looked out of the window and saw on the horizon, bearing
210°M, what appeared to be a planet. It had the colour and
magnitude of Venus but clearly could not be Venus. It then
became apparent that it was moving towards me and I assumed
that it was another aircraft showing landing lights flying
along an intersecting airway. The light became too bright ta
be an aircraft and as it approached one could see trails
behind it. (It is possible to see contrails by starlight).
These trails however appeared to have yellow sparks in them
and as it got closer several red lights, the size and colour

SME 40
27
of aircraft navigation lights, which kept station with the
bright light and behind it.

The closing speed was greater than that of an aircraft one
flight level above and the distribution of the lights much
wider and longer than would have been possible had it been
another aircraft, but too close for a formation.

It passed overhead and appeared on the starboard side with
the bright light unchanged and the red lights still keeping
station. In other words it could not have been landing
lights on another aircraft because it was far too bright and
they do not shine backwards, As it receded to the horizon
the "starlight" retained its colour but lost its intensity
until, at the horizon, it turned yellow and disappeared. We
were not sure if this was because it burned out or because
it disappeared into the haze layer.

The total time from horizon to horizon was four minutes,
The "trails" were visible only when it passed close
overhead,

The descriptions in the press of the space laboratory which
came down some years ago in Western Australia tally with
what we saw on the night of the 19th.

I hope that this report is of interest and value. My crew
and I would very much like to have an explanation if
possible,

Yours faithfully,

he cor engine fades to a spluttering halt. The
familiar night sounds cease abruptly on the
deserted roodway. And the twinkling lights in the

@ dazzling ship from another world,
The ramp lowers. A silvery form is sithouetted

cargo of one very frightened human being.

Hundreds of millions of viewers across the world
watched in TV-eyed amazement as this climactic scene
ended the 1987, and final, series of the American soap
opera The Colbys. Fallon (played by Emma Samms)
was taken for her ride into the sky, leaving programme
makers to decide whether her adventure was for real
of just a Pamela Ewing-style dream.

TV soaps seem cosily divorced from real life. We
soak in their glitzy images, knowing all the time they
are just make-believe. Yet this particular episode from
the Hollywood dream machine is not entirely without
foundation. in fact it is based ‘on an incredible
phenomenon that has swept across the USA and even
now threatens the shores of Britain.

People by the saucer-lood are claiming to have
beenkidnapped by those dazzling ships and corted off
to pastures new and decidedly extra-terrestrial, They
also say that dark and painful things are done to them
aboard these UFOs; even maintaining that genetic
experiments are carried out.

‘Of course this is ail very hard to swallow. But scien-
tists are beginning to take these cases seriously.

Take the story of 24-year-old Kathie Davis from
indianapotis. Then o young mother of two small boys,
she was living in a house at the edge of the city when,
onthe night of 30 June 1983, a strange light oppeared
over her backyard. From that point onwards memories
flowed stickily into one another, leaving in their wake

who cannot possibly know about the other cases —
report details that are absolutely identical with those

but to believe what they say?”

Dear Sir,
The purpose of my letter is to request your expert advice, and assistance.

Skyscan is a national organisation who investigate reports of ‘Unidentified
Flying Objects’ for the purpose of providing material for scientific study.

We do not perpetrate the mythology of ‘Flying Saucers’ and alien space men,
On the 15 February 1988, at 4.i5pm, SoC AO mre saw a
strange object in the sky above her home in Kings Lynn, Norfolk.

She described the object as of elongated shape (Like a rugby ball) with no
wing or tail surfaces. The object was painted black with, she believes,

orange undersurfaces, and it made a noise like an aircraft — or a motor-

cycle ¢

The object was travelling north/west, and came from the direction of Marham.
When above her house, the object turned towards the north, and at speed,
passed out of sight into broken cloud and mist.

Mrs Garfoot also said that the object wobbled gently, and appeared unstable.
I would greatly appreciate your help in this matter Sir.

L. Thank you for your letter of 25 May 1988 asking for any information we may
have on possible Unidentified Flying Object (UFO) sightings in the Ilkley area of
Yorkshire on 1 December 1987. I have searched through our files but regretably

can find nothing which relates to the incident you are interested in.

2. However you may find it helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive

and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the main concern of the
Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat to

the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they

do, and this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate

or identify the objects.

3. We have no department which is appointed solely for the purpose of studying
reports of UFOs and we have no staff who are employed on the subject full time.
This being so we do not have the resources to undertake any further investigation
or general survey. The sighting reports we receive, which usually are limited

to the brief details of the incident passed on to us by those who witnessed

them, are referred to our staff in the departments which are responsible for

the air defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports as part

of their normal duties.

ky Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications

we do not carry out full time studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have
the resources to undertake any indepth investigations. I am sure that you will
appreciate that defence funds are fully committed to the continuing security

of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This
being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investiga-
tions, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

5. Nevertheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange
things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explana-
tions could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities which spring

to mind include satellite debris re~entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning,
unusual cloud formations, meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft

at unusual angles amongst others.
6. Although we are able to answer any specific queries you may have about
particular incidents which have occurred recently and can let you know whether
we have a report on the incident and if so what it contains, I regret that the
release of any material on the subject "en bloc" is not possible as, like other
government departments all MOD files are subject to the Public Records Act.

As you may be aware, this Act states that official files generally remain closed
from public viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken.

7. I hope that you find this helpful.

Thank you for your letter of 15 May 1988 asking

South Yorkshire area on and around 20 April 1988.

I have looked through our files and am sorry to say
that we did not receive any reports which relate to
the details supplied in your letter.

I can also confirm that we are not aware of any flying
operations in the area concerned, although I cannot
discount the possibility that there might have been
the odd individual training sortie here and there.

I hope that this is of help.

Thank you for your letter of 18 June which asked for information
on any procedures that you might have to follow should you come face
to face with alien lifeforms in the vicinity of your house.

I think you will find it helpful if I first explain that whilst
we do receive and co-ordinate some information about Unidentified
Flying Object (UFO) sightings, the sole concern of the Ministry of
Defence is to determine whether or not they present a threat to the
security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that
they do, and this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD
to investigate or identify the objects. The sighting reports we
receive, which usually are limited to the brief details of the
incident passed on to us by those who witnessed them, are referred to
our staff in the departments which are responsible for the air
defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports as part
of their normal duties.

Although I would like to confirm that the Ministry of Defence
does not possess any positive evidence to suggest that "alien
spacecraft or beings" have landed on this planet, I feel that our
limited involvement with the subject prevents us from being able to
answer your question with any degree of accuracy. However, as this
could be considered to be more of an internal and perhaps, police
matter I am copying your letter to the Home Office.

I hope that this is helpful.

UFO_ TYPES

In order to differentiate concisely but adequately between the many UFO shapes
and types reported, the following catalogue describes and (for future reference)
codes the various forms presently recognized by Data Research. These codes :
appear in a few of the summaries and analyses, detailed on later pages. There
are a few new additions to the codes since our last publication of the UFO
Register as some of the witnesses descriptions could not be catagorised within
the existing list.

Flat disc: round, domeless.
" " multisided, domeless.
" " round, centrally domed. Domes variable in size.
" " or Dustbin-lid shaped: round, sub-pyramidal, usually domeless.
" " with Antennae
Hatshaped: central "cabin vertically cylindrical (but often rounded on
top) encircled ventrally by a flat rim or flange.
Bowl or Dish shaped: round, domeless.
" " " round, domed, or sometimes with central "spindle"
like device.

Saucer shaped: round, centrally domed. Domes variously proportioned.
Double saucer shaped: round, slightly convex units, joined peripherally.
we " " round, markedly convex units, similarly joined.

Rugby-ball shaped: domeless.
" " " centrally domed. Domes usually small or very small.
" " " with small ieteral fins.
Saturn-like: encircling flange joined to main orb-shaped body of object.
Sphere or Globe: Plain surfaced. Sometimes with "ports" or apertures.
" " " surface panelled or segmented.
" " " tailed. Tails of various shapes and lengths.
" " " domed. Domes usually very small; can be situated on
both the top and underside of main body of object.

Sphere: winged
"

with "feet"
Egg-shaped:
Round: a blanket description. Either types 1a to 4 viewed full-face,

or an indistinct manifestation of type 8a.

Elliptical or Oval: either that shape or oblique views of types ta-5a.
Oval: has body transversely bisected by bar-like structure.

" has elongated shape. Variant-possibly of type 42.
Pyramidal: pitch to apex variable (possibly a variant of type 1d)
Cone shaped: one end rounded. Often described as bullet-like or shell-

like (military, not molluscan shells are meant here).

" " as type 13a but with one end pointed.
Rocket-like: single or multi-finned.

" " finless.
Arrow shaped: length variable. Shaft or stem sometimes very short.
Torpedo shaped: (including those forms described as "sausage''-like).

" " with circular lump on one end.

Cigar shaped: (Including those forms described as dirigible shaped).

oN dorsally domed. :

" ", domed dorsally and ventrally.
Cylinder shaped: diameter uniform throughout length (including forms

described as "cigarette"-like or pole shaped).

" " wholly or partially tapered.
Funnel-like or Bell shaped: may be an acute variant of type 18b.
Torch shaped: cylindrical in main body but one end fanned-out.
Rod-~like: usually very thin and sometimes very long (possibly a variant

of type 18a).

Crab-like: possibly a variant of type 31
Flat triangle: domeless
" " : domed. Domes usually very small: number and positions of
same variable.
Diamond or Lozenge shaped: nearly always domeless and usually flat.
Oblong shaped: flat.
Square or Rectangular in shape: flat.
" " " ": six-sided or open box-like.
Diffuse or Cloud-like: believedly connected with one or more solid objects
obscured within. Cases are known where solid objects enter
or emerge from erratic (also stationary) moving cloud-like

masses.
Globular Lights: probably solid objects obscured by luminosity.
" " : tailed.

Star-like: probably solid objects obscured by luminosity.

Flare-like:

Tadpole shaped: may be connected to type 46a

Squiggly shaped or "blobs of light": highly flexible objects, often

altering shape continuously.

Railway-carriage shaped: (Minus wheels).

Jeep-like (minus Wheels): occasionally with a central turret.

Boot —shaped: may be connected with type 50 (see Stanway and Pace 1968. )
"Flying Saucer Report: UFOs Unidentified, Undeniable".P.11

House or Haystack shaped: apparently a roofed-in box-like form.

Generator-like:

Triglobular:
Bi-globular: as far as known only small size objects.

Grid-like: can be square, round, closed peripherally, or open bordered.
Twin or Multi-tailed: body usually oval or spherical, with two or more
j tails. :

Irregularly shaped: objects of solid construction but conforming to no
describable shape. Possible th se indeterminate
shapes are more a result of poor eyewitness reporting.

Bird shaped: (not mothman): usually gigantic, solid body of a general
avian configuration, with huge moveable "wings" (see Fort,
Wilkins, et al, for interesting reports of this UFO-type).

Propeller shaped: has three equally spaced, curved propeller-like
flanges round a disc shaped of globular shaped central
body.

Concentrated Beams of non~-dispersive light: Sources undiscernible.

triangular shaped superstructure.

CASE INTRODUCTION.

This report was first notified to the investigator on the
Tuesday following the witness’ sighting by the witness himself,
after having tried many official and non-official
organizations. Finally, having phoned a number of BUFORA
addresses, the witness was given the phone number of the
investigator. The investigation interview eventually took place
on Sunday, 26th June, 1988, at the home of the investigator in
Hampstead, London.

According to the witness, both he and his girl friend were
taking a mid-evening stroll in Headstone Manor Park when
suddenly, the corner of the witness' eye was caught by a bright
light low in the sky. When the witness made a full frontal
look, he perceived a large, flat charcoal-grey-coloured object
moving away in a southerly direction. After only having the
object in view for two seconds, it suddenly vanished in an
unaccountable way, however, he did manage to draw his
girlfriend's attention to the object who, only having only the
briefest glance at the object, did not appear to be very
impressed.

When asked what it was about his sighting that caused the
witness to believe that the object was unusual, he replied that
“the rear of the object displayed two large luminous greenish-
white concave-shaped "ports"". Also, he declared that the dark
body of the object was, in some way, “ribbed", and that,
despite its bulk and extremely rapid mode of departure, no
sound could be detected at all. Strangely, despite the fact
that it was still light during this high-summer evening, and
that there were other people about, there were no other known
witnesses or reports in the local media.

I would like to refer to your recent telephone enquiry on the
subject of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) when you asked for any
information that we might have appertaining to sightings seen in the
South East of England in the sixties and seventies.

I think you may find it helpful if I explain that whilst we do
receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the
main concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or
not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of the United
Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally the
case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the
objects.

We have no department which is appointed solely for the purpose
of studying reports of UFOs and we have no staff who are employed on
the subject full time. This being so we do not have the resources to
undertake any further investigation or general survey. The sighting
reports we receive, which usually are limited to the brief details of
the incident passed on to us by those who witnessed them, are
referred to our staff in the departments which are responsible for
the air defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports
as part of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we do not carry out full time studies into the UFO
phenomenon nor do we have the resources to undertake any indepth
investigations. I'm sure that you will appreciate that defence funds
are already fully committed to the continuing security of the UK
against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This
being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific
investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had
been identified. ,

Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that
many strange things have been observed in the sky, although we
believe that rational explanations could probably be found for most
of them. Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the
earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles

ty
or a host of other things.
I regret that the release of any material on the subject "en

bloc" is not possible as, like other government departments
files are subject to the Public Records Act. As you may be
this Act states that official files generally remain closed

all MOD
aware,
from

public viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken, ie
1958. Although material prior to this date could be available for

public viewing I regret that our UFO reports for the period
1967 were destroyed in line with our policy at the time.
Nevertheless we are able to answer any specific queries you

about particular incidents which have occurred recently and
you know whether we have a report on ‘the incident and if so
contains. However, due to limited resources, I regret that
instigate general searches through our files.

Thank you for your letter of 16 April 1988.

I have searched high and low for your letter of 7 March 1988 but unfortunately,

I have been unable to locate it. For this I apologise, either on behalf of MOD
Or perhaps more appropriately, on behalf of the Royal Mail - I do not recall ever
having received it!

If you would like to write to me again with the details of the incidents that you
are interested in I will, of course, see if we received any reports which relates
to them and if we do, provide you with copies.

Io regret, however, that because of limited resources and other pressures of work,

I will be unable to offer any indepth investigations or analyses of what might

have been seen unless, of course, the object was thought to have been a threat

to the security of the UK. However, I must assure you that this is not normally

the case. As you might already be aware, since our interest in UFOs is limited

to possible defence implications we do not carry out full time studies into the

UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources to undertake any complete investigations.
Tam sure that you will appreciate that defence funds are fully committed to the
continuing security of the UK against, primarily, agression from the Warsaw Pact
countries. This being so, we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific
investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

I hope that this is helpful.

Thank you for your letter of 18 April 1988 enquiring about Unidentified Flying
Objects (UFOs).

You may find it helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate
some information about UFO sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence
is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence
of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is normally the
case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.

The Ministry of Defence has no department which is appointed solely for the
purpose of studying reports of UFOs and we have no staff who are employed on

the subject full time. This being so, we do not have the resources to undertake
any further investigation or general survey. The sighting reports we receive,
which usually are limited to the brief details of the incident passed on to

us by those who witnessed them, are referred to our staff in the departments
which are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom and they examine
the reports as part of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications we do

not carry out full time studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources
to undertake any indepth investigations. I am sure that you will appreciate

that defence funds are fully committed to the continuing security of the UK

against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so,

we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations unless

a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange

things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite debris
re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host

of other things.
Although we do hold a limited number of report files on this subject, I regret

that the release of this material "en bloc" is not possible as, like other government
departments, all MOD files are subject to the Public Records Act. As you may

be aware, this Act states that official files generally remain closed from public
viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken. However, we are

able to answer any specific queries you may have about particular incidents

which have occurred recently and can let you know whether we have a report on

the incident and if so what it contains.

I hope that you find this helpful.

Thank you for your two recent letters and the enclosed
photographs. We are extremely sorry to hear of your difficulties but
have studied your photographs very carefully, and would assure you
that there is absolutely nothing on them to worry about.

More generally I would also like to assure you that although
foreign aircraft, some with unusual markings,do fly over the United
Kingdom from time to time, none do so without our knowing about it.
There is therefore no reason for you to be frightened; for yourself
or your daughter, about any aircraft that you see. Please also be
reassured that everyone here in the Ministry of Defence will continue
to work hard to ensure that all the people of this country are well
defended and have nothing to fear.

I hope you will find this helpful, and am returning your

photographs herewith.

pate /Sec (AS 12/3

& June 1988

i.
Thank you for your letter of 4 April 1988 to the Ministry of
Defence which has been passed to me for attention. You expressed an

interest in Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) and asked for any
information that we might have on the subject.

You may find it helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive
and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the main
concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not
UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of the United

Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally the

case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the
objects.

The Ministry of Defence has no department which is appointed
solely for the purpose of studying reports of UFOs and we have no
staff who are employed on the subject full time. This being so we do
not have the resources to undertake any further investigation or
general survey. The sighting reports we receive, which usually are
Limited to the brief details of the incident passed on to us by those
who witnessed them, are referred to our staff in the departments
which are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom, and
they examine the reports as part of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we do not carry out full time studies into the UFO
phenomenon nor do we have the resources to undertake any indepth
investigations. I'm sure that you will appreciate that defence funds
are already fully committed to the continuing security of the UK
against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This
being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific
investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had
peen identified.

Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that
many strange things have been observed in the sky, although we
pelieve that rational explanations could probably be found for most
of them. Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the
earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles
or a host of other things.

I regret that the release of any material on the subject “en
bloc" is not possible as, like other government departments all MOD
files are subject to the Public Records Act. As you may be aware,
this Act states that official files generally remain closed from
public viewing for 350 years after the last action has been taken. We
are however able to answer any specific queries you may have about
particular incidents which have occurred recently and can let you
know whether we have a report on the incident and if so what it
contains.

I hope that this is of help.

Dur &

Your reference

Our reference
D/Sec(AS)12/3
Date
‘7 May 1988

Thank you for your letter of 18 January which asked for
permission to inspect any of our departmental files which contain
reports of unidentified Aerial Phenomena sightings as seen and
reported to us by members of the public.

Whilst you are indeed correct in saying that our reports files
are not classified and like all other government departmental files
are subject to the Publics Records Act, I regret that the Ministry of
Defence does not possess any UFO sighting reports prior to 1967. As
was mentioned by Viscount Long in the House of Lords on 4 March 1962
all reports sent in prior to 1967 were destroyed after five years of
keeping. Since then, however, our policy has been to keep all
sighting records of UFOs that we have received but given the
provisions of the Public Records Act I regret these will not become
available for public viewing “en bloc" until at least 1997. However,
as you may already be aware, we are able to answer any specific’
question that you might have concerning a particular incident which
has occurred recently and if we have a report, let you know what it
contains.

You also asked if an applicant could have access to public
records prior to reaching the 30 year release point. I have
established that, within the MOD there have been certain occasions in
the past when individuals have been allowed access to unclassified
official documents before their official release date, mainly to
known historians but this is exceedingly rare. As you might expect,
each case is taken on its merits and all applications are very
carefully looked into. The branch within the Air Force Department
that deals with such matters is AHB(RAF) & PCB(Air) who can be
contacted at Room 413, Lacon House, Theobalds Road, London, WC1xX 8RY.

I hope that this proves helpful. I am sorry for the long delay
in replying.

Anns Sm cents,

AFIS 3

4% May 1988

Thank you for your recent letters which ask many questions on
the subject of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs). As you are
probably aware, the Air Staff Secretariat is responsible, amongst
other things, for this particular subject within the Ministry of
Defence. This being the case, you might not be surprised to hear
that your recent letters to RAF Leeming, RAF Finningley and HQ RAF
Strike Command, High Wycombe have all landed on my desk for an
answer. To avoid unnecessary repetition and duplication of work I
would be grateful if you would note that the Air Staff Secretariat is
the sole branch which members of the public should contact if they
wish to write about the subject of UFOs. Having said that, however,
I think I must emphasise that this particular topic is only a very
small part of my total workload and as such I am only able to devote
a small percentage of my time on it. I, therefore, regret, that I do
not have the necessary resources to undertake general searches for
information nor can I attempt to reply to questions that we do not
have the information to answer.

I hope that you will accept this as an interim reply. Iowill
try to look at the detail of your letters in the near future.
