j 7 i i inesby by
Near Chesterfield,
ch was made during the week commencing May th 1987. ‘The details are as follows.

At 9.45p.m. on Friday, May 9th 1967, FEM were travelling
by car in a northerly direction along a country lane running parallel to the M1 motorway
at a point half a mile south of junction 29, near Chesterfield.

Section 40] | spotted a silver coloured object flying from east to west over

the motorway. She immediately drew her husband's attention to it. The object was
bullet shaped, with a fluted rear section. It_had no wi showed no sign of
exhaust emission. As it crossed the notorway etl realised that the object
was losing height. I+% cleared the motorway at a height estimated to be no more than
twenty feet and continued on a downward trajectory.

Both Section 40, were under the impression that the object was coming
down into the fields on their left, although their vision of it was obscured as they
negotiated a slight left-hand bend in the road. By the time their car had cleared the
bend, the object was out of sight.

The witnesses have stated that this was unlike any aircraft they have ever seen,
in fact it bore greater resemblance to a rocket or a guided missile. Furthermore, the
terrain in the area is such that no aircraft” travelling at “Such a speed could have
pulled out of the steep dive without crashing into the hillside at Stainsby to the west
of the motorway.

The story was related to me on Monday, May 12th 1987; when Section 40) asked
me to accompany him on a search of the area.

The search was severely hindered by a tall crop of Rape in the field where it was
thought the object may have crashed or landed. It is true to say that an area of
vegetation in the middle of the field seemed to have been disturbed, although from the
perimeter there was no sign of wreckage or debris. As this was private property it
was considered unwise to proceed through the erop therefore the brief search concluded
with a visit to the nearby farm in order to inform the landowner that some kind of
projectile had possibly landed in his field.

rv consideration of the experience over a period of ten days,
a: the possible implications regarding national security and
public safety, if for instance the object had been some kin accidentally
released from an aircraft. It was at this point ‘net om 235 to
report the incident to personnel at R.A.F, Coningsby, this being an operational

station situated to the east (from which direction the object had travelled) and which
would most likely have the appropriate radar coverage to track such an object.
. *

contact him regarding the matter.

So far no contact has been made and there has been no explanation of this
strange event.

enquiries regarding the processing of his report. A note of authorisation is

I telephoned R.A.F. Coningsby at approximately 3.50p.m. on Monday, April 26th

to a member of the R.A.F. Police who informed me that there was no-one at R.A.F.
Coningsby who could assist me. I doubt that this is the case if
report was taken seriously and acted upon.

Although I respect the authority of the R.A.F. Police and I understand the need

REMSection 40. |

Dear Sir,

I am a Sheffield student and have an interest” in
observations of unusual aerial phenomena; for around ten years
I have been privately investigating instances of "UFO"
sightings in the South Yorkshire/Derbyshire area out of
scientific interest, and have recently begun to send my case
reports to the Secretariat (Air Staff) 2a at the MOD in London.

I was advised to write to you by Pilot Officer
of RAF Binbrook near Lincoln in reference to a number of
sightings of unidentified aerial phenomena in the
Sheffield/Rotherham area on the night of Tuesday, February 2nd,
1988, and on several subsequent nights. I have collected a
large number of independent sighting reports from this
particular night which appear to relate to the passage of a "V~-
shaped" craft-with a series of red lights over the northern
suberbs of Sheffield; it appeared to come from the Blackburn
Meadows/Rotherham area and passed over Ecclesfield where it was
seen and heard by police officers shortly before 8 p.m.

I have received reports from over 40 independent witnesses
in various parts of the northern suberbs of Sheffield
corroborating the details given above. I have been in touch
with RAF Finningley at Doncaster over this matter and they
suggested that a light civilian aircraft may have been
responsible; however, subsequent enquiries with Manchester,
East Midlands and Leeds/Bradford airports [copies of letters
enclosed] as well as with a large number of RAF/military
airbases have drawn a blank - no one appears to know anything
about what was over Sheffield that night.

I have also received reports from five groups of independent
witnesses who saw a very large diamond-shaped object, soundless
and covered with many coloured lights flying at very low level
in the area between Hoyland (Barnsley) and Penistone on the
night of Wednesday, February 10th, 1988. The nights of February
5th and February 18th also produced a number of reports of very
large structured craft carrying brilliant lights at low-level
from several parts of South Yorkshire.

Are you able to give me any suggestions or information about
what has been going on in the skies of South Yorkshire
recently, i.e. whether any military exercises, refuelling
manoeuvres etc could be responsible. I accept that a large
percentage of these observations can be explained in
conventional terms - i.e. bright planets, unusual
meteorological happenings, ete., but there remain many

cal See (AS) & Room. IE
sightings which cannot be accounted for so easily.

Many of the witnesses above are educated and responsible
people - including microbiologists, engineers, police-officers
and ex-RAF personnel, some of whom have expressed great concern
that these “objects” are flying so low over densely populated
areas.

I must add that I am interested in this subject out of
scientific curiosity, and feel that reports of such incidents
should be recorded and collated in report form in a way that
they could be of use to possible scientific investigation in
future years.

I'm enclosing a Stamped Addressed Envelope and look forward
to hearing from you in the near future,

Yours Sincerely,

Leeds Bacto Aircort Limited

Yeadon Leeds LS19 7TZ wesection40

14 March 1988

Thank you for your letter dated 3 March 1988. I am afraid that
we are unable to identify any aircraft that might explain your
sighting on 2 February 1988, or on the 10th February. The only
aircraft controlled by Leeds Bradford Airport at the time you indic-
ated was inbound to LBA from the west having departed from Dublin.
Your suggested track from Rotherham to Ecclesfield is not a route
likely to be used by aircraft operating to or from this airport.
Doncaster or Sandtoft airfields have, in the past, carried out
some night flying and may possibly have had traffic in the area
you mention.

The only aircraft that would route to Leeds Bradford from the
Sheffield area at the times you specify would fly either via the
airways system from the South and pass to the West of Sheffield,
near Penistone but usually above 4000 feet, or alternatively route
from the Finningley area passing well East of Sheffield.

Very low-level flights are more likely to be of military origin

but this would not occur over built up areas. “However, it is
not unknown for helicopters and other aircraft to operate at night
at fairly low level. In good visibility aircraft a long distance
away can seem close and of course you would not hear the noise.
However, we have no information about any military activities.

I am sorry I cannot be of any further assistance.

ours sincerely

Airport Director

Registered in England No. 2065958
Registered Office: City Hall, Bradford, West Yorkshire, BD1 1HY

15th March 1988

Dear Sir,

In reply to your letter of the 3rd March 1988 it would seem that the UFO seen on

the night of Tuesday, 2nd February 1988 would appear to have been travelling in a
westerly direction, in which case it is more than unlikely that it was an aircraft

en route between East Midlands Airport and Leeds Airport or vice versa. It would
seem more likely, if it was an aircraft, that it might be heading for Manchester
Airport or the aerodrome at Woodford, or might be low level military traffic in

which case the departure point and destination would be unknown to us. emg emphasis)

I can throw no light on the activities observed on Wednesday, 10th February 1988
in the area between Hoyland and Penistone, except to add that both Hoyland and
Penistone lie beneath airway Bravo 1.

I can only add, and I am sure that you will be aware, that lights seen at night
can be very deceptive in terms of distance or estimated distance, particularly on
nights when there “en visibility.

Yours fait lly,

Thank you for your letter dated 29 February 1988. We have investigated our
traffic patterns but are unable to confirm any movements.

You will no doubt be aware that Manchester is responsible for controlling
aircraft within Controlled Airspace and the nearest route to the area you
indicate would be some 10/15 miles to the North and flying at altitudes in
excess of 6,000 feet, mainly in an East/West direction.

I would remind you that aircraft both civil and military have flashing
navigation lights both red, white and sometimes strobe lights which give a
very strong flash/beam effect. These in conjunction with certain
cloud/weather conditions can provide unusual effects.

Yours sincerely

ATC Operations

in

Thank you for your recent letter which asked about our policy on the subject of
Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs).

I think it would be helpful if I explain that the main concern of the Ministry of
Defence is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security and
defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not
normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.

The Ministry of Defence has no department which is appointed solely for the purpose
of studying reports of UFOs and we have no staff who are employed on the subject
full time. This being so we do not have the resources to undertake any further
investigation or general survey. The sighting reports we receive, which usually are
limited to the brief details of the incident passed onto us by those who witnessed
them, are referred to our staff in the departments which are responsible for the

air defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports as part of their
normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications we do not
carry out full time studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources
to undertake any indepth investigations. I'm sure that you will appreciate that
defence funds are fully committed to the continuing security of the UK against,
primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so we could not

' justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations, unless a clear threat
to the security of the UK had been identified.

Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange things
have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations could
probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite debris
re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host

of other things.

YoUing ovfo wpe cond gabon

( I would like to confirm that we received approximately 150 reported sightings of
UFOs from members of the public during 1987.

I regret that the release of these reports "en bloc" is not possible as, like
other government departments all MOD files are subject to the Public Records Act.
As you may be aware, this Act states that official files generally remain closed
from public viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken. However,
we are able to answer any specific queries you may have about particular incidents
which have occurred recently and can let you know whether we have a report on the
incident and if so what it contains.

I hope this proves helpful.

Dear Sir,

Thankyou for your letter dated 23 March and for the very
helpful information enclosed, for which I am very grateful to
you. Thanks also for the comments on the sighting reports I
passed on to you, I will continue to send you the most
interesting of the sighting reports I receive, as I feel it my
duty to keep you informed about such happenings. Are you able
to give any comments specifically regarding the series of
photographs taken in Barnsley in August 1987? Does the object
in the photograph resemble any kind of aircraft/helicopter that
the MOD are familiar with, for example?

I have been continuing my enquiries into sightings of aerial
phenomena in the South Yorkshire area during January/February
of this year. As you probably will know, on the evening of
Tuesday, February 2nd, a large number of independent witnesses
in the northern suburbs of Sheffield and Rotherham saw a very
large object carrying a number of red and white lights flying
in an easterly to westerly direction, at very low altitude.

Following these reports I made enquiries with RAF Finningley
and was told that the object involved was probably a light
civilian turboprop aircraft flying between Leeds/Bradford and
East Midlands airports. On making enquiries with both these
airports, as well as with Manchester Airport and the RAF bases
at Church Fenton, Binbrook, Leeming and Linton-on-Ouse, I was
told that it definately was not an aircraft connected with any
of these places.

Section 40, oeaney of March 27 I informed Flight Lieutenant
of RAF Finningley of the above, as well as sending him ha, |
copies of statements from witnesses who had observed the objett .
on February 2nd and positively identified it as a low-flying
military aircraft. He told me he has "sent a copy of [my]
letter and of the enclosed sighting account forms to the MOD
department responsible for dealing with such reports".

Assuming you have received the above are you able to
identify the "UFO" reported by amongst others the police-
officers at Ecclesfield as a military aircraft? May I draw your
attention to remarks made by the Airport Director of
Leeds/Bradford airport viz “very low-level flights are more
likely to be of military origin". Also written at the bottom of
your report form relating to the Ecclesfield sighting are the
words "“low-flying aircraft/helicopter/piston-engined aircraft";
how was this ascertained as when I spoke to Pep section 40M
they were emphatic that what they saw was definately not a
conventional aircraft?

Also, in paragraph 2 of your letter you mention that it is
"highly unlikely" that an intruder of conventional origin would
be able to travel to the UK without being spotted. In view of

this stateme your attention to your statement to
my colleague dated March 15th that "the RAF are
not aware of any air defence activity on the dates and times in

bate in the South Yorkshire area" [referring to Feb 2 and
Feb 10].

As no airfield in the north of England has admitted having
these objects on radar and also that they were not flown from
any northern airfield it follows that, given your rationale,
the craft seen on Feb 2nd was in fact an unidentified flying
object as by your own definition it was not registered on
radar, I would like to hear your comments on the above.

I do of course understand your reasons for declining my
offer of assistance to investigate UFO reports in the north of
England. However, may I ask if it is possible for you to keep a
.record of our 24-hour answerphone service (Telephone: Section 40]
Section 40] aie to let witnesses who contact you know about this
service. Therefore, if they wish to speak to someone who is
able to spend time looking into their sighting (which you make
clear in your letter that you have no time or resources to do
this yourself), it can then be left up to them to get in touch
with us if they so wish (thus there is no problem about
confidentiality). I must point out that ail of our small group
of investigators each have many years of experience in the
investigation of aerial phenomena, and we like to set high
standards in our research,

Thankyou also for your information about the whereabouts of
the sightings reports from 1973-1974 which I asked about. Are
these "central archives" open to the public, and if not would
it be possible for my MP to view the information on my behalf?
The sightings I am referring to (of a "phantom helicopter"
repeatedly chased by police-cars in January 1974) I am
interested in due to their scientific interest from a
perceptual/sociological viewpoint.

I look forward to seeing your reply to the above [SAE
enclosed], and I would like to thankyou once again for your
invaluable help and co-operation.

Yours Sincerely,

Dear sir/madam,

I am writing to you on behalf of an organisation called 'Skyscan’.
This was formed in 1972, and is a nationwide network of neople who
investigate reported sightings of unidentified flying objects
(UFO's). I realise that you may regard UFO 'groups' as cranks in
the main, but let me assure you that Skyscan is a serious team of
people who work to very high standards. We know from experience that
the overwhelming majority of reports can be explained as known
phenomena (aircraft, meteors etc.), but there remains a amall number
that cannot be explained (at least by us - and we also refer these
unexplained reports to a panel of consultants to see if they can find
an explanation).

The reason for this letter is an enquiry. We have a number of reports
stretching from Castle Donnington over to Spalding in Line's for the
date of the 9th /IO0th December, 1987. We know of many witnesses who
reported seeing two -— three large objects with accompanying 'humming’
noise. When there are a large number of witnesses, it usually suggests
a simple explanation. We think that they ail saw a group of large
aircraft, probably military. While we realise that you may not be

able to be specific, we would like some form of confirmation of our
suspicions. If you cannot help, perhaps you can suggest an alterna-—

tive?

I thank you for your time, and look forward to hearing from you.

HEADQUARTERS MILITARY AIR TRAFFIC OPERATIONS
Hillingdos House Uxbridge Middlesex UB10 ORU

Dear Sir

' Thank you for your letter dated 28 March 1988 on
behalf of 'Skyscan' concerning reported sightings of
unidentified flying objects (UFOs).

This Headquarters does not investigate or gather

statistics on UFOs. We have therefore forwarded your

_ letter to the appropriate department at the Ministry
of Defence.

Yours faithfully

Squadron Leader

April 6th 1986,
Dear Sir/Madan, ;

I am at the moment investigating a most intrigueing
case. A man who lives in Ilkley has made certain claims concerning an
alleged UFO experience of the 4th Kind. These claims are currently being
examined by specialists in various scientific fields. However, it is an
incident which the witness has reported as occuring after the UFO event
which concerns me here.

According to the witness and his wife, two men arrived at his house
at approximately 8.50pm on January 15th, 1988. They wore smart business
suits, were in their forties and produced identity cards claiming they
represented the MOD. These cards were headed ‘MOD', beneath which were
photographs of the bearers, followed by their names. These were;

Section 40 jag someone called Section 4a

The men stayed for half an hour. Section 40, Rese most of the
questions, which were about the witness' UFO experience. Can you answer

the following questions please ?

1) Did two men from the MOD call on a man in Ilkley on that date ?

3) Do plain clothes investigators from the MOD visit witnesses to
alleged UFO events ?

4) Are identity cards held by MOD personnel laid out in the manner
described 7?

5) How could such MOD investigators have found out about the incident ?

Because of the calibre of this witness we are carrying out a
thorough examination of his claims, and would welcome your help in
throwing some light on the above incident.

Thank you for your letter dated 29 January 1988 to the Chief of the Air Staff,
Air Chief Marshal Sir David Craig. He has asked me to reply on his behalf.

We note and thank you for your suggestions concerning the purchasing of equipment

and products for the Ministry of Defence using the "United Kingdom and Commonwealth
Trading Yenture" as a suitable vehicle. Further to that I have pleasure in attaching
a small booklet entitled "Selling to the MOD" which explains the opportunities

open to small companies and the procedures to be followed if you wish to be involved
with defence procurement.

I hope that this proves heipful.

Your Reference

Our Reference
D/Sec(AS)12/3
Date

2x9 March 1988

Thank you for your letter of 28 January. I am sorry for the delay in replying.

The Ministry of Defence is very grateful for your kind offer of voluntary assistance
with the investigation and collation of information on the UFO phenomenon. As

you rightly say we do not have the manpower or resources available to conduct
in-depth studies or investigations ourselves.

However, as you may already know, the MOD's involvement with the UFO subject is
extremely limited in that our main concern is to determine whether or not UFOs
present a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we

judge that an unidentified sighting does represent a threat, and this is not normally
the case, the MOD does not attempt, as we feel there is no need, to identify or
investigate the object or objects further. The sighting reports that we receive,
which usually are limited to the brief details of the incident passed on to us

by those who witnessed them, are referred to our staff in the departments responsible
for the air defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports as part

of their normal duties. Those rare sighting reports which are considered to warrant
further attention from the point of view of being a possible threat to our continuing
security are also investigated in-house.

in essence, despite the merit in your suggestion, we feel that there is little
need Erom our point of view of an outside agency investigating UFO sightings on
behalf of the Department, and therefore must decline your offer of help.

Tam sorry to have to give you a disappointing reply and wish you luck with your

future endeavours with the investigation of the UFO phenomenon.
